Key Developments

Chernomyrdin Interviewed Following NDR
Conference in Ufa

LD1210145595

[FBIS Editorial Report] Moscow Russian Public Tele-
vision First Channel Network in Russian at 0540 GMT
on 12 October on the "Teleutro” program carries a six-
minute report on Ufa and the Russia Is Our Home
[NDR] conference held during a recent visit there by
Prime Minister Viktor Chernomyrdin. The program: in-
cludes a recording of an “exclusive” interview with
Chernomyrdin by an unidentified correspondent during
his visit to Ufa on 10 and 11 October.

Chernomyrdin is shown speaking to the correspondent
at a horse racetrack. He states: "You know, we have
this long-standing question, which is now an issue: how
much power we should keep to ourselves and how much
we should give to the regions. We agree that the Lord
God is not going to provide everything, especially with
the system that is being developed here.

"I have to say that we do have work to do at the federal
level and on the regional level. We must not be afraid to
make decisions on this, on the contrary. The thing is that
it has to be done here and they have to do it themselves,
locally. There really is work to be done, and we have
to get down to it. The most important thing is that it
should be in line with the law. The agreements being
signed have to be comprehensible and reasonable and
should clearly demarcate the tasks; and must be built up
within the framework of the Constitution.

"The deal, i.e., the agreement on the demarcation of
powers that we are signing today, on what we have to
do, is being done in line with the Constitution. We have
to do this more quickly, we have to be bolder in doing
it. As you know, I am convinced that we are on the
right track, that we have people who understand and
know what has to be done; but most importantly, what
must be done while implementing the Constitution and
what we have to do generally for the country, for the
great power. Strong regions mean a strong Russia. Do
we really have to oppose this"?

The reporter points out that Bashkortostan has been a
sovereign entity longer than Chernomyrdin has been
prime minister. Chernomyrdin responds: “That is right,
yes. Of course, I would have liked to have (?gotten
there) earlier, but I am convinced that now we have
come through the most difficult time. Painful and
difficult as it has been for us, the most critical, the crisis,
is over. I am sure that 1996 will be somewhat better for
us. Right now it all depends on us. We need calm, we
need considered, responsible and calm work.

DUMA ELECTION ISSUES 1

“We need legislation and we need our legislative work
to be quicker, we need to constantly improve and review
it, and, most importantly, all this will eventually enable
us to improve everything else. I am convinced this will
improve the growth in output and then there will be
political stability. This will, first, be reflected in the
economy, although a little improvement in the political
sphere would not do any harm either. I would not want
one area to harm the other. There are the people. Look."

Chernomyrdin’s welcoming ceremony at the airport is
then shown followed by a clip of him and Ramazan
Abdulatipov addressing a hall of people from the NDR
movement. The correspondent explains that for all the
debate over the relative positions of the regions and
Moscow within Russia, Bashkortostan is one of the most
prosperous of the regions in the Federation. Tatarstan
President Mintimer Shaymiyev is shown addressing
the meeting. Over video of people in the foyer, the
correspondent explains that Bashkiria is marking the
fifth anniversary of sovereignty.

Agrarians Collect 2 Million Signatures for
Registration

LD1410150595 Moscow ITAR-TASS in English
1414 GMT 14 Oct 95

[By Aleksey Subbotin]

(FBIS Transcribed Text] ULAN-UDE, October 14
(ITAR-TASS) — The Agrarian Party of Russia (APR)
has completed its signature campaign, collecting two
million signatures to registrate for the elections to the
State Duma (Russian parliament lower house), which
will be submitted to the Central Electoral Commission
on Monday, party chairman Mikhail Lapshin said here
on Saturday.

Lapshin starts a three-day tour of Buryatia today.

The APR is ready to put forth an initiative, right after
the elections to the State Duma, to start joint consul-
tations of left-wing, centre-left and centrist forces on
pinpointing a single candidate for the 1996 presidential
elections in Russia, Lapshin noted.

The Russian Communist Party, parties and blocs of
socialist orientation, trade unions and goods producers
can be participants in such consultations. The APR
leader stressed that left-wing forces cannot permit
themselves a luxury to act single-handed during the
1996 elections.

Lapshin’s programme of the Buryatian tour provides for
visiting agricultural districts and a speech at a republican
meeting of agricultural producers, which will open in
Ulan-Ude on October 16.

This report may contain copyrighted material. Copying and dissemination
is prohibited without permission of the copyright owners.

2 DUMA ELECTION ISSUES

Movement Created To Support Agrarian Party
Candidates

LD1310173495 Moscow ITAR-TASS in English
1536 GMT 13 Oct 95

(By Tamara Ivanova]

([FBIS Transcribed Text} MOSCOW, October 13
(ITAR-TASS) — A public movement has been cre-
ated to support candidates running elections from the
Agrarian Party of Russia (APR).

ITAR-TASS learned from the APR faction’s press ser-
vice that about 300 representatives of the agrarian and
industrial sector and public representatives from various
regions of Russia met members of the movement at the
Russian Ministry of Agriculture headquarters.

APR leader Mikhail Lapshin called the creation of the
movement as a “logical step in the development of the
Agrarian Party," whose membership, as he said, now
stands at 350,000 people. The main task of the move-
ment is to lend multilateral support to APR candidates
on the party’s general federal list, as well as single man-
date electoral districts. The movement’s representatives
are expected to form a powerful "supporting group" of
the agrarian and industrial sector, manifesting intense
activity at all stages of its election campaign from col-
lecting signatures to held election propaganda.

As far as collecting signatures is concerned, the press
service of the agrarian and industrial sector said that this
work is about to be over and by the beginning of the next
week the sector plans to submit the required number of
signatures to the Central Electoral Commission.

Disinformation Campaign Seeks To Blacken Lebed,
KRO

MMI1610141695 Moscow PRAVDA in Russian
14 Oct 95 p4

[Article by Maxim Goloviev: "Shot in the Back. General
Lebed Is at War Again. Information War")

(FBIS Translated Text) A few days ago the editorial
offices of a number of periodicals received some cam-
paign leaflets with very curious contents. The leaflets
depicted a grinning General Aleksandr Lebed assuring
the electorate: "I will teach you to love the motherland."
It gets better — the leaflet (if you believe the text) be-
longs to the People’s Labor Alliance [NTS], which is
apparently appealing to people “to give their votes to
deputies who are running for the State Duma on the
lists of the Congress of Russian Communities [KRO],
and first and foremost for General Lebed." The leaflet
ends with a clear-cut statement — "Russia needs his
firm hand."

FBIS-SOV-95-199
16 October 1995

Given that the NTS has denied that it participated in
the preparation of this information material, if you
could call it that, the leaflet could be shrugged off
as someone’s mad ravings if this had been the only
fabrication. Incidentally, the leaflet was quite simply full
of errors (maybe deliberate?): It contains an appeal to
vote for "deputies running for the State Duma," whereas
anyone who knows anything whatsoever about politics
knows that only candidate deputies can run for the
Duma.

However, the surprises do not end with the leaflet.
A certain "INFRA-M news research bureau" (to be
blunt, we are dealing with an obvious newcomer, at
least so far no one had ever heard of this bureau)
cizculated a report according to which, "from a well-
isformed source close to the top leadership of the
KRO," the INFRA-M news research bureau has found
something out. According to this something, "a few
days ago Gen. A.I. Lebed had a meeting shrouded in
secrecy with the leadership of the NTS, which has
long been known for its collaboration with the German
Wehrmacht during the war (through Vlasov’s army)
and the Western special services (the Secret Intelligence
Service, the CIA, and the Bundesnachrichtendienst) in
the postwar period." The source close to the KRO
leadership presented INFRA-M with the information
that the NTS’s proposals on the participation of the NTS
in Gen. Lebed’s Duma election campaign, with sights
set on the 1996 election campaign, were discussed at the
meeting. In order to achieve this aim, the NTS center
located in Frankfurt am Main allocated an automobile
(a lavish present) "for Lebed’s closest aide in the 14th
Army," Colonel Anatoliy Platitsin, and "provided for the
manufacture in Germany of advertising materials (T-
shirts carrying Lebed’s portrait, posters, matches and
lighters carrying Lebed’s portrait, and also a special
consignment of ‘General Lebed’ cigarettes). [no closing
quotation mark]

You get the impression that someone very much wants
to discredit Gen. Lebed and the KRO. It would seem
that the method that has been chosen is not a new one,
but one from the 1930’s — pinning on Gen. Lebed
the label "spy of all intelligence services." Meanwhile,
the target of the forgeries is fairly subtly conceived.
The words "I will teach you to love the motherland"
attributed to Lebed are designed to frighten away the
socialist associations which are part of the KRO. At the
same time, the "exposed" tie between Lebed and the
NTS would put the patriotically minded voters on their
guard and trouble them.

In this connection it is worth noting an article in
NOVAYA YEZHEDNEVNAYA GAZETA entitled
“Whom Did Lebed Call." The article constitutes a

This report may contain copyrighted material. Copying and dissemination
is prohibited without permission of the copyright owners.

FBIS-SOV-95-199
16 October 1995

report by Police Lieutenant Colonel S.V. Karasev,
deputy chief of the RSFSR Service for the Security
and Protection of Government Installations, in which
A. Lebed is "convicted" of allegedly not protecting the
"White House" but wanting to seize it in August 1991.
Evidently the article is supposed to drive apart the
participants in the August 1991 defense of the House
of the Soviets on the one hand and the KRO and Lebed
on the other. The first sentence in the article stands out:
"People are collecting signatures ‘for Lebed’ and ‘for
Rutskoy’ at the Taganskaya subway station." This is a
blatant lie — people do not gather signatures for both
at the same time, because the KRO and “Derzhava"
are political rivals. The conclusion is also very strange
— that, having such compromising material on the
serviceman, you can order him both to storm the "White
House" in 1993 and fight in Chechnya. Meanwhile,
Gen. Aleksandr Lebed, as we know, has nothing to
do with either because he was commander of the 14th
Army in the Dniester Region at the time.

We have here the fabrication of compromising material
on Aleksandr Lebed, and the forms, methods, and
ploys are irrelevant. It will not be at all surprising if,
before long, we obtain "information" to the effect that
the general traded in poor quality consumer goods or
something along those lines. Some people are irritated
by the fact that the political rating of the KRO and one
of its leaders — the fighting Gen. Lebed — is constantly
rising, and that people are turning up at the public
reception rooms set up by the KRO, having despaired of
meeting with understanding and support in the offices of
high-ranking officials. And since it is impossible to shut
the congress up, attempts are beginning to be made to
discredit its leaders. However, it should be remembered
that it is hard to find dirty linen where there is none.

Pensioners Claim Coercion Into NDR Support

964F0064A Moscow PRAVDA ROSSII in Russian
5 Oct 95 p3

[Articie by Anatoliy Belozertsev: "Signature or Bread!"]

{[FBIS Translated Text] Our correspondent in
Chelyabinsk received a telephone call from Salakh
Rakhimyanovich Karipov, an invalid of the Great
Patriotic War, who recounted the following:

"The wife and I are advanced in years. We leave home
just to go for a walk. Food is brought to us by Nina
Filippovna Bryabina, an employee of the Sovetskiy
Rayon social assistance center. We thank her for this.
But then last Friday she came to us at home, set out
some bread and other food, and suggested to my wife (I
was not home) that she sign the signature list for Russia
Is Our Home [NDR]. My wife refused to do this.

DUMA ELECTION ISSUES 3

"The whole thing was repeated again on Monday, this
time in my presence. I also categorically refused to sign.
Then Bryabina angrily declared: “What, do you not want
me to bring you bread in the future?’ It is difficult for
me to say what was concealed behind these words—
a threat, or fear of losing a job. But the fact that the
administration of the rayon is compelling its workers to
collect signatures in favor of pseudo-reformers who are
ruining our country disturbed me and my wife. We were
also disturbed by the fact that, besides the seven names
that were already there, our last name, patronymics, and
first names, our address, and even passport data had
already been written in without our knowledge. All that
had to be done was for us to sign. Fine, we understood
what was what, but, after all, there are a lot of people
among our brother pensioners, and especially invalids,
who can be easily duped. I think that it is dishonest
and even immoral to collect signatures for the ‘party in
power.”"

To satisfy myself of the authenticity of what S. Karipov
said, I visited the Sovetskiy Rayon people's social
assistance center. Assembled in the office of Director
G. Dorogovaya were her deputy, V. Kamenev, and the
supervisor of the sector for assistance to invalids at
home, M. Saprykina. All three saw nothing illegal in
the fact that they were obliged to collect signatures for
NDR. But they doubted very much that their employee,
N. Bryabina, had done this by frightening pensioners. |
had to get Karipov’s home telephone number. After a
difficult conversation with the war invalid, the managers
of the center became convinced that their employee had
Stepped over the line.

"Well, they collected signatures, but what does that
mean? Even so, almost all pensioners declare that they
will vote for the communists,” M. Saprykina frankly
confided to me.

It would be very nice if this note were to be seen by
the managers of the Central Electoral Commission and if
they would give a legal evaluation in PRAVDA ROSSII
of the collection of signatures by workers of the people's
social assistance center who are directly subordinate to
the local authorities.

Grachev Sets Up Electoral Commission at Defense
Ministry

LD1310154795 Moscow Radiostantsiya Ekho Moskvy
in Russian 1300 GMT 13 Oct 95

(FBIS Translated Text] A special commission has been
set up at the Defense Ministry on the instructions
of Pavel Grachev. The commission will organise the
elections in the armed forces, AFP reported.

This report may contain copyrighted material. Copying and dissemination
is prohibited without permission of the copyright owners.

4 DUMA ELECTION ISSUES

At least 100 high-tanking Russian officers are to stand
the December parliamentary elections, and the commis-
sion will have to select candidates and then to streamline
the voting process in the armed units, a Defense Min-
istry source said.

Defense Minister Grachev did not sign any documents
on the setting up of a commission to organise the
elections at the Defense Ministry, Pavel Grachev’s press
secretary Yelena Agapova said in an interview with our
radio station:

[Begin Agapova recording] One document has been
prepared and exists in reality. The document concerns
the improvement of legal standards among both voters
and organizers of the elections in the armed forces. The
document mainly describes who should explain what,
and it only concerns the technical side of the elections. It
is explanatory work, and nothing more. [end recording]

IZVESTIYA Apologizes for ‘Inaccuracy’ in Earlier
Report

MMI1210122795 Moscow IZVESTIYA in Russian

12 Oct 95 p2

{IZVESTIYA Editorial Office "Correction"]

(FBIS Translated Text] An IZVESTIYA article on 30
September this year, entitled “Nikolay Ryzhkov Allies
With Jordanian Editor of Anti-Semitic Newspaper,” re-
ported that Shaaban Khafez Shaaban [name as translit-
erated] has not acquired citizenship of the Russian Fed-
eration. As S. Altapov, chief of the Moscow City In-
ternal Affairs Visas and Registrations Administration,
has stated in an official letter to the editorial office, this
does not correspond with the facts and V. Tikhomirov,
a staffer of the Visas and Registrations Administration,
who was quoted in the report, did not provide that in-
formation.

We apologize for the inaccuracy in our report.

Election Guidelines

Daily Explains Law Regarding Double Candidacies

MM1110121695 Moscow ROSSIYSKAYA GAZETA
in Russian 7 Oct 95 p6

[{"Explanation of the Procedure for Applying Article
42, Section 10 of the Federal Law ‘On the Election of
Deputies to the State Duma of the Russian Federation
Federal Assembly,’ Approved by Russian Federation
Central Electoral Commission Decree No. 23/198-P
of 3 October, followed by commentary by Valentin
Maslennikov, under the general headline "Frankly"]

{[FBIS Translated Text] Article 5 of the Federal Law
"On the Election of Deputies to the State Duma of
the Russian Federation Federal Assembly" stipulates
that 225 State Duma deputies are elected from single-
seat electoral districts and 225 State Duma deputies are
elected from the federal electoral district.

Under Article 6 of the Federal Law "On the Election
of Deputies to the State Duma of the Russian Federa-
tion Federal Assembly” the right to nominate candidates
belongs 1) directly to voters, and 2) to electoral associ-
ations and electoral blocs.

Under Article 37 of this law electoral associations and
electoral blocs can nominate the same person as a
candidate both by putting him on the federal list of
candidates and by nominating him for a single-seat
electoral district.

Under Article 20 of the Federal Law "On Basic Guar-
antees of Russian Federation Citizens’ Electoral Rights"
candidates for deputy nominated directly by voters par-
ticipate in elections as independent candidates and can-
not belong to any electoral association or electoral bloc.
Under Article 37, Section 3 and Article 57, Section 4
of the Federal Law “On the Election of Deputies to the

This report may contain copyrighted material. Copying and dissemination
is prohibited without permission of the copyright owners.

FBIS-SOV-95-199
16 October 1995

State Duma of the Russian Federation Federal Assem-
bly” a person’s membership of an electoral association
or electoral bloc signifies his nomination as a candidate
for deputy by that electoral association or electoral bloc.

Consequently the requirement of Article 42, Section
10 of the Federal Law "On the Election of Deputies
to the State Duma of the Russian Federation Federal
Assembly” applies only to candidates nominated for a
single-seat electoral district by an electoral association
or electoral bloc.

A person on the registered federal list of candidates
nominated by an electoral association or electoral bloc
cannot be registered by the district electoral commission
as a candidate nominated directly by voters.

Commentary. Law Against Tricks in Fight for
Deputies’ Seats

The nearer the elections get the more questions their
organizers have. The process of sorting out federal
lists of candidates for deputy from electoral blocs,
associations, and parties is in full swing. By the middle
of last week the Russian Federation Central Electoral
Commission [CEC] had already registered 78 such lists.
At the same time lists of single-seat candidates are being
finalized directly in districts. As soon as these registers
became the property of the public at large, people were
immediately puzzled as to why the same surnames often
crop up in both lists. Can that be right? What does the
law say about this?

The Federal Laws "On Basic Guarantees of Russian
Federation Citizens’ Electoral Rights" and "On the Elec-
tion of Deputies to the State Duma of the Russian Fed-
eration Federal Assembly" say clearly and unambigu-
ously that all electoral associations and blocs, as well
as voters directly, have the right to nominate candidates.
These laws also expressly stipulate who can or cannot
be on both lists simultaneously.

For instance, the same person cannot be put on federal
lists of various associations or parties. A contender for
a deputy’s seat cannot be put simultaneously on one
list as an independent candidate and on another as the

representative of a party.

How can that be? Because Article 42, Section 10
of the federal law on the election of State Duma
deputies runs: "The same person can be

simultaneously as a candidate on the federal list and
as a candidate for a single-seat electoral district.”
But this provision, the CEC explains, applies only to
candidates nominated for a single-seat electoral district
by an electoral association or bloc. That is, the column
denoting who nominated the candidate must without
fail indicate the name of his "parent" association, bloc,

DUMA ELECTION ISSUES 17

or party. If instead of this it is indicated that he
was nominated directly by voters as an independent
candidate, that would be a clear case of deceiving voters.
Which the law does not allow.

Some elect!on organizers and voter activists are con-
fused by the lines of Article 57 of the law on the election
of State Duma Deputies which say that an independent
candidate can also indicate his party membership on
the voting paper. But you will agree that belonging to a
party and being nominated by it for the next parliament
are different things. The law is against any misrepresen-
tation and is against trading on anybody’s authority for
mercenary purposes.

Zyuganov Interviewed on Elections, Possible
Alliances

BR1310123695 Madrid EL MUNDO in Spanish
13 Oct 95 p22

{Interview with Russian Communist Party leader Gen-
nadiy Zyuganov by Francisco Herranz in Moscow; date
not given: “The Elections Will Be Rigged Massively"
— first paragraph is EL MUNDO introduction)

([FBIS Translated Text] Moscow — Gennadiy
Zyuganov, a Sl-year-old “apparatchik,” is “num-
ber one” in the Russian Communist Party, a powerful
organization with half a million members. From his
office at the Duma, he spoke with the authoritativeness
derived from representing the Russian political force
that may obtain the largest number of votes and seats
in the general elections scheduled for mid December.

{Herranz] Do you think that the 1996 presidential
elections will be held without any fighting or coups?

[Zyuganov] Everything that has been happening over
the past few years is a “putsch," first to destroy
the Soviet Union and now the Russian Federation.
They have created the basis for a very serious social
explosion: 20 million unemployed, 15 million people
starving, 6 million refugees. These are not reforms, but
fully-fledged genocide.

We are witnessing an unstable equilibrium. The Kremlin
is aware that it cannot continue implementing these poli-
cies, but it is not powerful enough to oust the bankrupt
ministers who represent the path of treason: Anatoliy
Chubays (privatization), Andrey Kozyrev (foreign pol-
icy), and Sergey Shakhray (nationalities).

{Herranz] In the event of the elections being rigged,
how are you planning to fight back if the people are
discontented but are passive?

pop pd ng oop material. Copying and dissemination
is prohibited without permission of the copyright owners.

18 DUMA ELECTION ISSUES

[Zyuganov] The people are tired and discontented, but
they are increasingly harboring deep feelings of hatred
which may explode violently. Why did Yeltsin

the election of the governors and of the regional
parliaments? Because he is afraid.

The elections will be rigged on a massive scale. This

before when they falsified 9 million
votes in favor of the new Constitution in the December
1993 referendum.

{Herranz] Might Yeltsin postpone the elections, seeing
they are doomed to failure?

[Zyuganov] He might possibly, but it would be difficult.
Our president usually gives his word and then breaks it.
However, it is impossible to call off the elections just
like that. He cannot do that, but he might be able to
do so with a provocation like the one in Budennovsk
(a hospital with 1,000 people in it held hostage — EL
MUNDO editor’s note).

{Herranz] How do you interpret the proposal to unify
Belarus and Russia?

[Zyuganov] It may be used as a pretext to postpone
the presidential elections. There are several possibilities
that could be used for this purpose. The first has taken
place in the Caucasus; the second in central Asia,
through the extension of presidential powers until the
year 2,000. There is a third possibility, too: adding up
all the violations and irregularities and decreeing that
the results are not valid.

{Herranz] With whom are you prepared to form an
alliance?

[Zyuganov]} Our conditions are very simple: Russia’s
territorial integrity and peace and well-being in every
home. We are prepared to reach a compromise with the
Agrarians and the parties of Ryzhkov (patriot), Kokov
(centrist), and even Yavlinskiy (liberal). With such a
parliamentary majority we would propose a popular-
confidence government in order to overcome the crisis.

{Herranz) You are accusing the Russian and Western
democrats of destroying the USSR, but are you not to
blame, too?

[Zyuganov] I blame myself for not having had the
strength and the time to unmask the clique headed
by Gorbachev and Yeltsin, who led the country to
disintegration. On the other hand, I used to have a
fairly good opinion of Western democrats, but they
all kept silent when the tanks arrived and fired on
our parliament. They have different ideals. Is Yeltsin a
democrat? Look at his features. Democracy never dwelt
for a moment on his face. Listening to him talk about
the Serbs is a hair-raising experience.

FBIS-SOV-95-199
16 October 1995

{Herranz) Are you planning to take part in the presiden-
tial elections?

[Zyuganov] The Russian Communist Party is in cavor
of limiting the president’s powers, but it intends to use
this post to strengthen peace in Russia and carry out
reforms. What will be my role? Whatever role I am

assigned.

Daily Comments on Gonchar’s View of Election
Scene

MK1310145595 Moscow KOMMERSANT-DAILY
in Russian 6 Oct 95 p 3

[Commentary by Natalya Arkhangelskaya: "Fried Foods
From the Kremlin Kitchen")

(FBIS Translated Text) The pre-election landscape
painted by the experienced hand of Senator Gonchar
would only gain from adding some details and partic-
ulars the author was somewhat sparing with. This is
quite understandable: What we have here is a political
intrigue being discussed only among insiders. Also
rather understandable is the outsiders’ burning interest
in it: Everyone has to eat the food cooked in the
political kitchen. KOMMERSANT-DAILY [KD] offers
the readers its own version of what is going on.

Beyond a doubt, as a successor to President Yeltsin,
Premier Chernomyrdin would quite suit both the United
States and Europe. But according to KD information, the
West regards as good prospects in this sense two further
figures: Moscow Mayor Yuriy Luzhkov and Congress
of Russian Communities [KRO] leader Yuriy Skokov. It
is said that these variants were the subject of discussion
in one of the recent meetings of Western politicians
in one of the European capitals. Allegedly even this
idea was voiced: If the premier and the mayor can
manage their election campaigns with their own funds,
the former Security Council secretary could very well
be given some help (let’s leave to Western analysts the
question of how the latter two, and especially Skokov,
could take the helm of the country). The following is
curious: the aforementioned exchange of views took
place shortly before the well-known press conference at
which Yeltsin suddenly came up with scornful remarks
about [Russia Is] "Our Home,” which, according to him,
will win 7 percent of the votes at best in the future
elections.

Much has already been said about the president’s being
unhappy with the premier’s growing political weight,
but by all indications no sharp moves in regard to
Chernomyrdin will be made. The president will hardly
dismiss the premier, since that would only boost his
election chances. The rumors of resignation are ULeing

: { may tai ighted ‘ jal. Copying and disse inati
is prohibited without permission of the copyright owners.

FBIS-SOV-95-199
16 October 1995

vigorously fanned despite the denials, while Skokov is
being named as a possible successor. And this may fully
explain everything.

Rumors about Skokov soon being appointed head of
the cabinet are cropping up with enviable regularity;
this rumor is the fourth to date. People who know
the former Security Council secretary in person do not
rule out that these rumors — like those about Skokov
and Yeltsin continuing to trust each other — could be
spread by Skokov himself (possibly with the support of
some persons close to the president). The objective is
to sell the desirable for the real. Nor does this version
clash with information contained in a recent “Itogi"
television program that the KRO leader, when in the
United States, promised President Yeltsin guarantees of
personal security in the event he came to power. It is
very doubtful that the president asked Skokov for any
guarantees. Most probably, the latter did this of his own
accord.

However, the information fuss surrounding Skokov may
also have yet another explanation: Rumors of his future
premiership are being launched with the objective of
causing a split in the KRO leadership. It is not hard to
guess who is lannching them. This version is attested to
by the zeal with which mass media outlets friendly to
the president fell to hyping this story. The main target
in this case is no longer Skokov — who would not be
able to elbow his way to the helm of power without
someone helping him despite the "psychological attack"
he is undertaking — but General Lebed. Lebed for
Yeltsin is indeed a serious problem to which, persons
in the know argue, the Presidential Security Service is
struggling to find a solution. According to information
from a source close to this special service, one version
of the "neutralization" of the general seems to have been
around for a long time. KD wrote that Lebed was asked
to become head of all Cossack forces in exchange for
deciding against running in June 1996. If he agrees, he
will have under his command large military subunits
without being subordinate to the defense minister, only
taking orders from the supreme commander in chief,
the president. According to information from informed
sources, the general has given his preliminary consent.

But this project seems to be held as an alternative
because circumstances may emerge under which the
authorities would think it good to hold no presidential
election at all. This is, by the way, something Gonchar
does not rule out either. A pretext for deciding against
elections, as has already been hinted at in the press,
could be provided by an accelerated process of the
unification of Russia and Belarus. Gonchar personally
contributed to the implementation of this project: It is
on his suggestion that the Federation Council passed a

DUMA ELECTION ISSUES 19

declaration the other day calling upon people in Russia
to say what they think in a referendum. According to
KD’s information, great efforts are being made to have
Kazakhstan join the new federation. One can of course
treat this with great skepticism. Finally, hints emerged
in recent days that the authorities did not quite give
up the idea of postponing the parliamentary elections
as well. The trial balloon is already up: State Duma
Speaker Rybkin noted when he started speaking about
extending (by presidential edict) the term of regional
bodies of power, that it would be logical to do the same
in regard to the federal authorities. And as is known, it
is often the Kremlin that we hear when Rybkin speaks.

{[FBIS Translated Text] A warning strike is in effect
today at all coal enterprises of the Kuzbass [Kuznetsk
Basin Coal Fields). It mainly differs from such previous
strikes in that it is taking place at the height of an
election campaign. Therefore, the strikers will hardly
limit themselves to the usual set of well-known slogans:
pay wage arrears, stop rising unemployment, and adopt
a government program to end the crisis in the sector.

Representatives of the coal industry workers’ trade
union, who are taking no part in the election campaign,
have stated that they are against "drawing the workers
into all sorts of political games." Their opposite numbers
in the Independent Miners’ Trade Union (IMTU), on
the contrary, are not going to let the moment slip, and,
taking advantage of the strike, they wanted to organize
an election meeting in Prokopyevsk, where canvassing
would be carried out for the IMTU leader, Aleksandr
Sergeyev, a candidate for State Duma deputy from Svy-
atoslav Fedorov’s Party of People’s Self-Government.
Furthermore, it is assumed that the Communists and
Liberal Democrats intend to put forward their own slo-
gans at some mines.

A study of the political alignment of forces in the
Kuzbass conducted by the Institute of Comparative
Studies of Labor Relations has indicated that the high-
est popularity rating of 11.7 percent is enjoyed by the
“Women of Russia" sociopolitical movement, followed
by the CPRF [Communist Party of the Russian Fed-
eration] with 6.2 percent, and Svyatoslav Fedorov’s
party in third position. According to the same study,
Kemerovo Oblast Soviet Chairman Aman Tuleyev has

from the operations of financial companies where they
deposited money, sounded as election propaganda".

INTERREGIONAL AFFAIRS 21

Gas Contract Signed With Bosnian Muslims, Serbs

LD1310170895 Moscow ITAR-TASS World Service
in Russian 1416 GMT 13 Oct 95

(By ITAR-TASS correspondent Igor Ivantsov]

(FBIS Translated Text] Moscow, 13 Oct (ITAR-TASS)
— About 100 million cubic meters of natural gas
will reach Sarajevo from Russia by the end of the
year, ITAR-TASS was told by Yuriy Zaytsev, acting
managing director of the Russian foreign-trade company
Gazeksport.

A contract for this amount of gas was signed today after
talks in Moscow, at which Bosnian Muslim and Serb
representatives were present, he said.

During the four days of talks, the sides confirmed the
total amount owed for Russian gas supplies since 1991,
which, Yuriy Zaytsev said, was put at $105 million. A
protocol on how and when the debt will be paid off
was also agreed during the talks. Each side quantified
its share of the debt, but it is thought that the Bosnian
Government will foot most of the bill. Payments are
expected to begin as early as this year.

Russian gas started flowing to Sarajevo again on 9
October. It was cut off last August at the request of the
interested parties. Sarajevo is currently being supplied
with sufficient gas to match consumers’ needs.

‘Concern’ on Bosnia Cease-Fire Non-Observance
Noted

LD1410112595 Moscow ITAR-TASS in English
1116 GMT 14 Oct 95

(By Valeriy Sevryukov]

{[FBIS Transcribed Text) MOSCOW, October 14
(ITAR-TASS) — “Russia has more than ample grounds
for serious concern over the non-observance of the
ceasefire agreement in Bosnia, reached on October 12,"
the Russian president’s special representative in former
Yugoslavia Aleksandr Zotov said in an exclusive
interview with ITAR-TASS here on Saturday.

The Russian side is one of the most active members
of the Contact Group, thanks to the actions of which it
was possible to reach the agreement, the diplomat noted.
For instance, it was largely achieved thanks to a quick
and constructive decision on Russian gas deliveries to
Sarajevo.

The special envoy stressed that "it would have been
next to impossible to reach this agreement without
this circumstance”. Its disruption threatened to be a
considerable roll-back from the viewpoint of prospects
for a peaceful settlement, which neihter Russia, nor
other members of the Contact Group wanted to happen".

Although the combat operations in Bosnia are of a local
nature, the diplomat continued, they are developing in
a very dangerous direction, resulting in an additional
number of casualties and refugees.

The sides, especially the Moslem-Croatian coalition, try
to gain “additional points" by expanding the scale of the
tragedy at this stage of a settlement.

As for Croatia, the Russian side cannot understand,
in principle, the presence of that republic’s troops in
Bosnia after the agreement was reached. A great danger
of provocations persists under these circumstances.

Taking this into consideration, Russia is extremely
worried over a prospect for a unilateral reaction of the
world community in the form of a force pressure only
on one side in the conflict.

Zotov expressed hope that Zagreb, although it did not
participate in concluding the agreement, will eventually
display the necessary will to achieve peace in former
Yugoslavia.

Zotov: Contact Group To Rationalize Efforts in
Bosnia

LD1510140595 Moscow ITAR-TASS in English
1335 GMT 15 Oct 95

[By Valeriy Sevryukov]

(FBIS Transcribed Text} MOSCOW October 15 (ITAR-
TASS) — A Contact Group meeting in Moscow, sched-
uled for October 16-17, is expected to rationalise efforts
to achieve a peaceful settlement of the conflict in the
former Yugoslavia, Aleksandr Zotov, the Russian pres-
ident’s special representative for the former Yugoslavia,
has told ITAR-TASS in an exclusive interview.

Zotov said Contact Group members may discuss such
documents which “would round off the period of the
critical development of the situation and would mark
the beginning of peaceful construction and gradual re-
integration of the entire region into the European struc-
tures". The point is, in particular, to seal and develop
principles for a state set-up of Bosnia-Herzegovina and
to tackle the humanitarian aspects of a settlement of the
situation in the former Yugoslavia.

Since each member of the Contact Group would like
to become the organiser of one of the elements in a
series of such activities, it can be expected that the next
phase of working meetings will take place in the United
States at the end of October and be held under the talks-
at-close-quarters formula, Zotov said.

This means that the sides involved in the conflict will
be able to devote attention not so much to clearing up
relations with one another as, upon gathering in one
place, to discussing constructively peaceful settlement
problems with international community members as
represented by Russia, the U.S. and the European Union,
Zotov pointed out.

France and Britain also actively advocate the holding
of Contact Group meetings in their capitals, with
the French side being intent on securing that the
Paris meeting sums up political measures within the
framework of the peace process.

As far as military aspects of the peace process are
concerned, Russia’s stand comes to a sufficiently clear-
cut determination of a U.N. mandate, under which
exclusively multinational forces would be used with an
explicit task assigned to them. Provided such conditions
are met, Russia would be prepared to send its military
contingent to the former Yugoslavia, Zotov said.

This report may contain copyrighted material. Copying and dissemination
is prohibited without permission of the copyright owners.

FBIS-SOV-95-199
16 October 1995

"On the whole, we regard the peace process with
cautious optimism, bearing in mind that it is still too
fragile so far. This is why the Russian side would like
to quicken its pace and expects that the Moscow meeting
of the Contact Group will stimulate to the utmost and
signify the practical start-up of accords and actions to
promote peace in the former Yugoslavia," Zotov pointed
out.

Russian-NATO Meeting Discusses Bosnia Peace
Plan

LD1510171695 Moscow ITAR-TASS in English
1656 GMT 15 Oct 95

[By Aleksandr Mineyev]

{FBIS Transcribed Text] SHAPE [Supreme Headquar-
ters Allied Powers Europe], October 15 (ITAR-TASS)
— A delegation of the Russian General Staff came to
the headquarters of NATO Allied Command Europe to-
day to attend the planning of the multinational peace
implementation operation in Bosnia and Herzegovina.

The delegation is led by Lieutenant-General Leonty
Shevtsov.

U.S. allied commander General George Joulwan intro-
duced to the Russians NATO commanders and military
delegates to its headquarters of the alliance’s 16 mem-
ber nations.

Joulwan led Shevtsov into his office to show him the
map of the former Yugoslavia, saying that the region
will be their common concern in the winter ahead and
that he hopes for Russia’s cooperation in establishing
peace in the region.

Joulwan told reporters that NATO welcomes at its
headquarters the delegation of Russia, a country with
important roles in Europe and in the world.

“We truly are one team focused on one mission — peace
in all Bosnia," Joulwan said.

General Shevtsov voiced the hope that the joint planning
would result in "an acceptable mechanism for participa-
tion of non-members of NATO" in the peace implemen-
tation effort.

Shevtsov said he and Joulwan had a general discussion
of the military aspects of the future peace-policing
operation in an initial hour-long talk.

He stressed that this will be “an operation of multina-

tional peace implementation force in Bosnia and Herze-
govina", not just NATO’s action.

The talk was attended by Russian Ambassador to
Belgium Vitaliy Churkin.

NATO Secretary General Interviewed on Bosnia

LD1510235095 Moscow Russian Public Television
First Channel Network in Russian
1900 GMT 15 Oct 95

(From the "Sunday" program]

([FBIS Translated Text] In an exclusive interview with
our program earlier this week, NATO Secretary General
Willy Claes insisted that the probability of a dual
command and the subordination of peacekeeping forces
in Bosnia were out of question.

[Begin recording] [Aleksey Pushkov, head of the ORTV
press service, in English with superimposed Russian
translation] Mr. Secretary General, what is your opinion
on the proposal made by Russian Defense Minister
Grachev that rotating command of the multinational
forces by the NATO and Russia should be established
in Bosnia?

(Claes, in English with superimposed Russian transla-
tion] Our military are not very enthusiastic about the
idea. They think that such a command will not be very
effective. They prefer cooperation between the military
structures to the principle of rotation. This is the mil-
itary viewpoint. Personally, I am not a military expert
and can only tell you what I have heard from our gen-
erals.

[Pushkov] At present the question of Russia joining the
peacekeeping efforts in Bosnia is being discussed. What
options do you see?

[Claes] First of all, let me say that in order to implement
the peace agreement in Bosnia, which I hope will soon
be concluded, Russian participation is necessary in all
cases. There is a number of reasons for that. Russia is
a member of the UN Security Council. It also actively
Participates in the work of the Contact Group. Finally,
Russia has long had close links with at least one of
the sides participating in the conflict. Therefore, there
is nothing to argue about. We are prepared to cooperate
with the Russians. You must also not think that we
got involved in all of this simply to support one of
the sides in Bosnia. We intend to play a neutral role,
but impose our rules of the game on all three warring
sides. If one of the sides breaks the agreement, and I
do not know who that might be, we will immediately
take measures to ensure a complete compliance with the
rules of the games and a successful implementation of
the peace agreement. So, we are prepared to cooperate.

This report may contain copyrighted material. Copying and dissemination
is prohibited without permission of the copyright owners.

26 RUSSIA INTERNATIONAL AFFAIRS

But I would like to make it perfectly clear. The peace
agreement cannot be implemented on the principle of a
dual key. We also insist that the peacekeeping efforts
must be carried out according to the NATO rules of the
game.

[Pushkov] Do you think that at present Russia represents
a danger to the Western alliance or to Central and
Eastern Europe?

[Claes] No, we do not consider Russia as being a threat.
Not at all. This is why we are trying to strengthen
cooperation in various fields, bearing in mind Russia’s
size and its potential as a European country, although a
large proportion of its territory is in Asia.

(Pushkov] Do you accept the idea of Russia becoming
a member of NATO, and if so, what will happen to
NATO?

[Claes] Frankly speaking, we have not discussed the
question of which particular countries will be accepted
into NATO. So, the inclusion of Russia into the bloc
cannot be ruled out. But I cannot give a positive answer
to your question. I simply do not know the answer. If
you ask me about the possibility of including any other
country into NATO, I will give the same answer. But,
as far as I know, full membership in the NATO is not
on Moscow’s agenda at the moment.

[Pushkov] In its latest documents NATO does not rule
out accepting in its ranks countries bordering with
Russia. Does it mean that there is a possibility of, for
example, the Baltic republics joining the NATO, and
that in such a case, and in certain conditions, of NATO
troops or even nuclear weapons possibly being deployed
on their territory?

[Claes] Once again, I would like to stress that we have
not studied the question of which countries will be
accepted into NATO. Because this question has not been
discussed, one cannot rule out the possibility of any
country. And another point. The more some politicians
in Moscow criticize the expansion of NATO, the less
results it will bring, and the more some countries will
knock on my door and ask for the expansion process to
be speeded up. I am not sure what is the reason, maybe
because of fear of Russia. [end recording]

delegation is due in Cuba later in October to study

good, then Russia is ready to start extracting oil in the
region, sharing the risks and the profits,” the agency
said.

In 1996, Russian specialists will help complete construc-
tion of a pipeline linking Matanzas port and a refinery
being built at Cienfuegos. Also, “in the coming months"
Russia will help plan the completion of the Cienfue-
gos refinery and the refurbishment of another refinery
at Santiago de Cuba.

A number of Russian banks and oil industry organiza-
tions are interested in these projects, Nidzelskiy said.
The Cienfuegos refinery alone, which he said will be

Trade Agreements Signed; Visit Ends

LD1610052095 Moscow ITAR-TASS World Service
in Russian 0022 GMT 16 Oct 95

(By ITAR-TASS special correspondents Oleg Velichko
and Konstantin Zhukovskiy]

(FBIS Translated Text] Havana, 16 Oct (ITAR-TASS)
— The official visit of to Cuba of Oleg Soskovets,
first deputy prime minister of the Russian Federation,
ended on Sunday [15 October] with the signing of
eight documents aimed at the development of bilateral
commercial and economic relations.

In Havana’s Palace of Congresses that evening
Soskovets and Carlos Lage Davila, vice president of
the Cuban Council of State, signed a protocol on trade
for 1996-98 envisaging reciprocal supplies under the
oil-for-sugar scheme, an intergovernmental agreement
on tourism, and also a memorandum on the results of
the talks which took place.

The Russo-Cuban documents testify to the qualita-
tively new stage of cooperation between the two states,
stripped of ideological basis, Soskovets said at the
solemn ceremony. In his opinion, the Cuban Govern-
ment’s desire to embody the idea of integrating the is-
land into the world economic system is today finding
real fulfillment. Reviewing the outcome of the Russo-
Cuban talks, he stressed that the sides had fulfilled the
instructions given by the leaders of the two states.

In the last few days Russo-Cuban relations have expe-
rienced very important moments, Carlos Lage Davila
said. Both countries are showing a resoluteness to ful-
fill the signed documents. Close economic ties and their
complement — commercial relations, feelings of friend-
liness, and respect — are the basis of successful devel-
opment of bilateral relations.

A number of inter-departmental documents were also
signed at the ceremony, concerning cooperation in the
fields of commerce, transportation, power engineering,
and scientific and technical cooperation. The sides
signed letters of exchange extending a 30 million credit
(units not specified] allocated by Russia for mothballing
the atomic power station being built here in Juragua.

Soskovets Trip Seen as Message to U.S.

MMI1610085795 Moscow IZVESTIYA in Russian
14 Oct 95 p3

[Report by Yevgeniy Bay: "Soskovets Brings to Havana
Our Response to Washington"]

(FBIS Translated Text] Toasts can once again be heard
in Havana in honor of indestructible Cuban-Russian
friendship. Oleg Soskovets declared Castro a strategic
ally and everyone said in unison that the former
misunderstandings are a thing of the past.

Soskovets brought with him to Havana a personal
message from Yeltsin and a 40-man team of government
Officials, bankers, businessmen, and military personnel.
They spent almost a week touring and clambering
around the whole island, visiting all the monuments
to past cooperation that have been cast to the will of
fate and begun to rot — the unfinished nuclear power
station, the abandoned nickel plant, and the neglected
super-harbor.

During their travels the Russian leaders recalled that
during the era of fraternity, Russia also got things from
Cuba. And that was the plain truth. In addition to a wide
range of citrus fruits Cuban "Partagas" cigarettes, along
with three-year-old and one-year-old “Havana Club"
rum (the latter was, admittedly, rather like methylated
spirits), were sold in our country for those with a cast-
iron palate. There were also candies and marmalade
made from the tropical guava.

However, there should already be an abundance of all
these products in Russia’s stores! Because back in late
1993, the creation of the joint "RosKuba” superconcern
was announced, comprising all the giants of national
industry, including the Siberian oil giants, a major
Russian airline, leading banks, and food importers.

But two years on the joint Russian-Cuban economic
front is only represented by one small joint enterprise
manufacturing chocolate candy, and it’s the Spanish
who call the shots there.

What about marmalade! The two countries have for
years been unable to perform the simplest of barter
operations — swapping oil for sugar. Russia hopes to
receive in October 1995 some of the sugar that we
should have gotten in early 1994, while this year’s
contract will be deferred to late 1996. Those responsible
for the deal, who won the right to buy raw sugar from
Cuba — Menatep-Impeks and Alfa-Ako — put in a
tremendous amount of effort and money to persuade
the Russian press that Cuban raw sugar is the cheapest
in the world. Now they cannot agree the price at which
the sugar bought from Cuba will be sold to the state.

This report may contain copyrighted material. Copying and dissemination
is prohibited without permission of the copyright owners.

28 RUSSIA INTERNATIONAL AFFAIRS

In 1993 Cuba was allocated a $350-million loan to
complete the unfinished installations abandoned by the
Soviet Union for natural reasons. So far the Cubans have
not received a cent of this money. Finance Ministry
leaders shrug their shoulders: The problem of Cuba’s
debts to Russia has not been resolved.

Nor was Castro prepared to resolve this problem during
his meeting with Soskovets. The visitor was only told:
We will talk when Cuba has restored its economic
potential. But he was asked to talk about Russia’s debts
immediately. Defense Minister Raul Castro reminded
Soskovets that Moscow has to pay the lease on the
electronic tracking station, whose task is to keep an
eye on the Americans. The Cubans are demanding $200
million, but Russian leaders cannot even so much as hint
that this money should be offset against Cuba’s debts,
which run to over $20 billion.

They cannot do so because the whole scenario for
Soskovets’ visit would be upset.

I remember that Russian leaders not so long ago tried
to persuade the world that there would be no return
to military cooperation with Cuba. Now there is talk in
Havana of the resumption of military ties. Indeed, when
NATO is declaring its readiness to move eastward, why
should we not go westward and reinforce our previously
abandoned redoubts?

Fidel is happy that the Russians who betrayed him have
returned to once again offer friendship. Soskovets also
feels good: We are not obliged to do as the United States
says, we are doing what we want.

Needless to say, that is the main thing, and we will
somehow manage without the sugar, the citrus fruits,
and the rum.

Fuel Minister Hails Decision on Caspian Oil Routes

LDI1610112295 Moscow ITAR-TASS World Service
in Russian U939 GMT 16 Oct 95

(By ITAR-TASS correspondent Igor Ivantsov]

(FBIS Translated Text] Moscow, 16 Oct (ITAR-TASS)
A win for Russia is how Yuriy Shafranik, Russian
minister for fuel and power engineering described the
decision of the international oil consortium to transport
the “early Caspian oil" along two routes — Georgian
and Russian. He told ITAR-TASS that this decision
properly takes into consideration Russia’s interests and
provides the chance to realize the capacity of the Baku-
Groznyy-Novorossiysk Pipeline, which has already been
built. “This is a good decision for Russia because we
have the pipeline, it is in working order, and is already
being used partly to pump oil from Chechnya”, said the

FBIS-SOV-95-199
16 October 1995

Shafranik repeated what he had said earlier, that there
was no other alternative to the Russian option of
transporting oil and the economic benefit for all is
obvious. He added that the Russian option has much in
reserve and thus “it has a realistic chance of being used
for transporting the bulk of the oil from the Caspian.”

The minister also said that there is no need to reassess
the consortium’s decision. "We should not call it historic
because this consortium of international oil companies
will not be the only one in the region." According to
him, plans for several more consortia to develop other
oil fields on the Caspian shelf are already being worked
on by Azerbaijan and foreign partners.

This is why Russia has responded with reserve to the
“raging passions” on the eve of the consortium’s deci-
sion, Shafranik said. “Russia is still a great power, we
have a ready option for transportation, great influence,
and wide-ranging contacts in this region so we do not
need to worry,” he added.

Two-Pipeline Solution for Azerbaijan Oil
‘Temporary’

MMI1610112295 Moscow ROSSIYSKAYA GAZETA
in Russian 14 Oct 95 p15

[Article by Aleksandr Krasulin: "Where There’s a Whiff
of Oil You're Bound to Find Americans. The United
States Wants to See a Steady, Uninterrupted Supply of
Azerbaijani Oil to Western Markets")

(FBIS Translated Text] As has been reported before,
the leaders of an international consortium engaged
in developing oil deposits in Azerbaijan reached a
compromise on Monday — the Azerbaijani oil will be
transported by two routes: a northern, via Russia, and
a western, via Georgia and Turkey. The initial stage
of development will be served by a Russian pipeline
to Novorossiysk, while the second pipeline will be
commissioned later.

Essentially the decision on oil pipeline routes was dic-
tated by Washington. U.S. administration representa-
tives have repeatedly insisted of late on the need for
several options for the delivery of Azerbaijani oil to
Western markets. Not long before the session of the
Azerbaijan International Operating Company [AIOC]
steering committee, at which this question was tack-
led, U.S. President Bill Clinton recommended that the
consortium approve the aforementioned routes, and on 2
October he had a nearly 30-minute telephone conversa-
tion with Azerbaijan President Geydar Aliyev. The U.S.
President urged Aliyev to support his proposal on the
choice of these two routes, actually stressing that this
might help reduce political instability in the region.

This report may contain copyrighted material. Copying and dissemination
is prohibited without permission of the copyright owners.

FBIS-SOV-95-199
16 October 1995

Officials in the White House refused to tell journalists
what Aliyev said in reply. At the same time, THE NEW
YORK TIMES saw the U.S. President’s appeal as an
attempt to pressure the Azerbaijani leadership, which
has to ratify the decision.

The new stage in the struggle for Caspian oil unfolded
following the discovery of three deposits containing an
estimated 511 million tonnes of recoverable stocks. This
is more than has been extracted in Azerbaijan in the past
45 years. One result of this was Azerbaijan’s signing
last September of a contract with the international con-
sortium which took three years to prepare. Participants,
terms, interest rates flickered past as in a kaleidoscope,
mirroring the succession of rulers and ruling clans....

The project was conceived under President Mutalibov.
It was given a political flavor by President Elchibey,
leader of the People’s Front. From the very beginning he
entertained the idea of laying a pipeline across Turkey,
and his plan was that Western companies would extract
the oil. But when Aliyev came to power he demanded
a new look at the terms of the contract, which, in
his opinion, was detrimental to Azerbaijan’s national
interests as it stood.

The region’s countries naturally had no intention of
standing by while the enormous oil pie was divided
up. Turkey became involved in the great political game
for Caucasian oil immediately after the breakup of the
USSR. It was prompted by the rather complex economic
Situation in that country.

Iran also had pretensions to participation in the contract.
Azerbaijan was intending to transfer five percent of its
block of shares to it for supporting the new interpreta-
tion of the status of the Caspian. But the United States
was resolutely opposed to its participation, so Iran was
left out of the deal of the century.

Analysts in Washington believe that the fate of the
Azerbaijan project will now determine whether the
development of the region — one of the world leaders
in the energy supply sphere in the next century —
is going to be pro-Western or something else. On
the pretext of protecting U.S. interests, therefore, the
Clinton Administration is publicly opposing Russia in
the Caspian basin and trying to accuse Moscow, which
currently has control of all the pipelines linking this
region to the Western market, of wanting to establish
control over the former Soviet republics.

According to those behind the project, on the one
hand the compromise involving the construction of two
pipelines on different routes will guarantee continuous
supplies of oil to Western markets irrespective of Kur-
dish activity in Turkey and the situation in Chechnya,

RUSSIA INTERNATIONAL AFFAIRS 29

where the northern branch will pass. On the other hand,
it is a gracious gesture to Russia, which still has strong
influence in the region and could hinder or even stall
the consortium’s activities for many years.

Nonetheless, the compromise is more of a temporary
nature, and once they have gained a foothold in the
region Western firms will find it easy to abandon it. As
consortium head Terry Adams said immediately after
the decision, in the long term an oil pipeline via Georgia
and Turkey to one of the latter’s Mediterranean ports
will be the preferred option.

Zatulin: Giorgadze’s Extradition, Azeri Oil
Connected

LD1410173295 Moscow Radiostantsiya Ekho Moskvy

{[FBIS Translated Text] Konstantin Zatulin, head of the

Talbott’s Visit Seen as Narrowing ‘Differences’

LD1310171595 Moscow INTERFAX in English
1602 GMT 13 Oct 95

[By diplomatic correspondents Aleksandr Korzun, Igor
Porshnev, Yevgeniy Terekhov, and others: From the
"Diplomatic Panorama" feature]

(FBIS Transcribed Text) MOSCOW, Oct 13 (INTER-
FAX) — Russia and the U.S. are intensifying coop-
eration in the Balkans. In connection with this two
U.S. deputy secretaries of state will participate in the
Moscow session of the Contact Group on Bosnia, to be
held on October 16-17, a high-ranking Russian Foreign
Ministry official told INTERFAX Friday.

According to the diplomat, Richard Holbrooke, in
charge of peaceful settlement in former Yugoslavia,
and also First Deputy Secretary of State Strobe Talbott,
known to be President Bill Clinton’s top adviser on
Russia, will come to Moscow.

The diplomat believes this demonstrates that Washing-
ton intends to maximize efforts in Bosnia so that an
international conference on the issue may be possible
as early as this year. This "fully corresponds with our
own interests," he added.

In addition, the Foreign Ministry official said that Tal-
bott’s visit is an attempt to narrow differences between
Russia and the U.S. "It is no secret that relations be-
tween Moscow and Washington have noticeably cooled
after NATO air strikes in Bosnia carried out regardless
of the Russian position,” said the diplomat. He sug-
gested that in the run-up to the U.S.-Russian summit
scheduled for late October Washington decided to set-

tle the disputes peacefully.

FBIS-SOV-95-199
16 October 1995

"Questions of the development of Russian-Ukrainian co-
operation in accordance with the accords reached dur-
ing the recent meetings between Russian President B.N.
Yeltsin and Ukrainian President L.D. Kuchma, and also

Chita Proposes Russia-PRC Border Dispute
Settlement

MKI1410150795 Moscow SEGODNYA in Russian
13 Oct 95 p3

[Report by Oleg Kryuchek: "Chita Is Proposing Its Own
Way of Settling Border Disputes Between the Russian
Federation and the People’s Republic of China. Joint
Economic Activity is Proposed on the Russian-Chinese
Border")

{[FBIS Translated Text] The Chita Oblast administra-
tion, on its own initiative, has proposed to the Russian
Foreign Ministry its own way of settling territorial dis-
putes that arise in the course of demarcation work in the
eastern section of the Russian-Chinese border. Having
given up the practices of its Khabarovsk and Maritime
neighbors, who keep claiming that the current demarca-
tion is a mere ceding of age-old Russian lands to China,
Chita is suggesting that a framework intergovernmental
agreement be worked out and signed on joint economic
use of the territories of Russia and China that are given
to either of the sides as a result of demarcation. Specif-
ically, it is suggested that an experiment be carried out
on Mengeseli Island (70 km long, seven km wide, with
an area of 17,500 hectares) on the border river of Ar-
gun, which is to be transferred to Chinese jurisdiction
under the demarcation terms.

According to the latest information, the Russian For-
eign Ministry has paid very serious attention to Chita’s
proposal, and recently Russian diplomats sent a note
to PRC officials containing a justification for joint eco-
nomic use of Mengeseli Island. The note emphasizes
that experimental joint economic activity on the dis-
puted territory should further promote good-neighborly

RUSSIA INTERNATIONAL AFFAIRS 31

Russian-Chinese relations and preserve the forms of
economic activity traditional for people living near the
border. Russia is pointing out that this territory should
be used entirely for traditional economic purposes, such
as haymaking, cattle grazing, farming, and wood cut-
ting, with all kinds of commercial and intermediary
activities banned. To simplify the procedure of cross-
ing the border, the Russian Foreign Ministry suggests
that local dwellers be admitted to the island with their
national domestic passports and identity cards or with
single-entry or multi-entry passes.

Your SEGODNYA correspondent has been told at
the Chita Oblast administration that the Chinese side
has treated the Russian proposals "with great attention
and understanding,” since the proposed model could
become a universal one in the settlement of all territorial
disputes. The text of an intergovernmental agreement is
already being worked on in Beijing.

Deputy Foreign Minister Views Relations With
Poland

WS1610145695 Poznan WPROST in Polish
15 Oct 95 pp 69-70

{Interview with Russian Deputy Foreign Minister
Sergey Krylov by Waldemar Gontarski and Iwona
Parchimowicz in Moscow, date not given: "A Lesson
In Democracy"]

(FBIS Translated Text] [WPROST] Does Russia, by
suggesting new principles of economic integration to
former CEMA members, intend to turn Poland and other
countries in our region into a sphere of influence?

[Krylov] These suggestions should not be seen as an
attempt at recreating CEMA in its former shape. There
are neither the circumstances nor the will. We are not
considering the establishment of a new domineering
structure. Poland and Russia both want to develop eco-
nomic cooperation. Your embassy in Moscow, headed
by Stanislaw Ciosek, whom I greatly respect, is very ac-
tive in this respect. If 80 percent of our relations were
economic and 20 percent — political, we would have
the proper balance. I wish to ask my Polish friends: Are
you against developing economic ties? Do you not want
Polish products to have easier access to the Russian mar-
ket? Would you not prefer for more Russian products
to reach Poland and Europe? I am talking, naturally, of
competitive goods which cost less because they do not
have to be exported a long way and their production
is cheaper. Tell me, are you against developing huge
joint economic projects? If you do not agree with all
this, why do you want to purchase fuel carriers from
Russia which are less expensive than those offered by
other states, for instance in the Middle East? Why was

This report may contain copyrighted material. Copying and dissemination
is prohibited without permission of the copyright owners.

32 RUSSIA INTERNATIONAL AFFAIRS

Poland so eager to approve of the idea of the Russian
vil and gas pipeline worth $2.5 billion which also cre-
2 >s a huge number of new jobs?

[WPROST] Do you not think that the document is vague
and thus invites the danger of far-fetched interpreta-
tions? It deals with the economy, ecology, and cul-
ture but fails to provide suggestiqns for creating mutual
banks and does not say a word on protecting transac-
tions and investments against risk, political included.

{Krylov} If you claim such solutions are lacking, let

your experts prepare them. Perhaps we really have
overlooked something. Let us talk.

[WPROST] How do the suggestions of economic rela-
tions between the Russian Federation and former CEMA
members fit in with GATT, the Central European Free
Trade Agreement and contacts with the EU?

{Krylov} They fit in very well. Poland’s and Russia’s
top priority is to approach the EU. Approximately 40
percent of our economic turnover is with EU states.
Once the agreement on cooperation and friendship
becomes effective, this figure may rise to 60 percent.
Initial talks ave under way on the establishment of a free
trade zone by states of the Central European Initiative.
The combined population of these states is 110 million,
plus 160 million people in Russia. Just think of the
benefits of a free exchange of goods and services on
this territory.

[WPROST] The proposal of the Russian Foreign Min-
istry coincided with the signing of “Russia’s Strategy
Towards CIS States" by President Boris Yeltsin. The
Russian press claims this strategy consists of combin-
ing economic and military cooperation, and its ultimate
objective is to create a counterbalance to NATO.

[Krylov] It is well known that we are against the
expansion of the alliance; we do not want it to approach
our borders. Considering that until recently our closest
neighbors were part of the same state, their tendencies
to restore the ties that were broken off over the last few
years are natural.

[WPROST] The new provisions combine the economy
with military cooperation, security issues and the secret
services.

[Krylov] Security has thus far been seen exclusively
from the military perspective in line with the belief that
the more tanks and planes there are the imore solid the
foundations of peace. In reality, we can speak of real
security when we do not have organized crime, drug and
arms trade, robbery. The secret services deal with that.
The secret services of our countries should cooperate in
these fields.

FBIS-SOV-95-199
16 October 1995

[WPROST] What political concessions is Russia expect-
ing from Poland in exchange for tightening economic
cooperation?

[Krylov] We are not even alluding to any political
concessions. Please understand that times have changed
completely. There is no longer the type of cooperation
we had 20 years ago, when Moscow dictated the rules.

[WPROST] Is the Russian veto to the enlargement of
NATO not an example of the old form of contacts?

[Krylov] No. The Poles are saying: NATO should
expand. We reply we do not want the alliance to accept
new members. Currently, unlike during the 1970s, both
sides can voice their opinions openly.

([WPROST] During his visit to Warsaw, President
Yeltsin first agreed to NATO expansion — only to
radically change his opinion later on.

[Krylov] Years go by and the Poles always focus on the
same problem, endlessly repeating the same question.
A year and a half ago I had the pleasure of talking to
President Walesa. He asked me the very same question
and added: "I will not ask Moscow whether Poland is to
enter NATO." I replied: "Thank you very much for these
words, for taking the burden of deciding about Poland’s
membership in NATO off Russia’s shoulders."

[WPROST] Nearly all political persuasions in our
country want Poland to join the alliance. Why is the
Kremlin against these aspirations?

[Krylov] I can agree all the Polish political forces want
to join NATO, but I cannot agree with the opinion that
all Poles share this wish. You have not yet calculated the
costs of joining the alliance. Do you know how much
it costs to support one soldier in the Western states?
Equipping, training and supporting one soldier in the
alliance costs an average of $100,000 annually — far
more than is spent in Poland on the same purpose.

[WPROST] How much will we have to pay for the
Russian mafia?

[Krylov] To counteract it you do not necessarily have
to enlarge NATO. It is enough if [Polish Interior
Minister] Milczanowski visits Moscow and [Russian
Interior Minister] Kulikov revisits Warsaw more often.
You cannot restrict yourself to talks between politicians
and the military. Economists and experts in various
fields should be invited to take part in the debate,
together with demographers — who would say what
the social structure shall be 10, 15 years from now.

[WPROST] The Russian papers have been suggesting

lately that in exchange for concessions made by NATO
to Russia on the Conventional Armed Forces in Europe

is prohibited without permission of the copyright owners.

FBIS-SOV-95-199
16 October 1995

[CFE] treaty, Moscow will agree to the enlargement of
the alliance to include the states of Central Europe.

[Krylov] A few months ago we presented our sugges-
tions on the CFE treaty because the document had been
developed back in the days of the USSR. The political
geography of the time was quite different. We are sat-
isfied that our suggestions have finally been heard. We
have no intention of showing off our muscles to anyone.
We do not wish to threaten anyone. We have gathered
too many tanks in Siberia, so you can have them if you
want, providing you transport them at your own cost.
Speculations that NATO might back out on the issue
of the conventional armed forces or that Moscow might
change her opinion on the enlargement of the alliance
do not lead anywhere.

RUSSIA INTERNATIONAL AFFAIRS 33

[WPROST] Is Russia democratic enough not to be
suspect of evil intentions?

[Krylov] Democracy is most important, real democracy
and not chaos. We do not yet know what democracy is.
We have never lived in a democracy. That is why we
also resort to imperial methods. Yet we will definitely
learn. The learning process could be shortened if we
did not have to be irritated by various issues, for
instance whether Poland is to join NATO and what the
president had said on the topic two and a half years
earlier. We will learn about democracy more quickly if
we concentrate on constructing pipelines, fighting the
mafia, or guaranteeing that more Hortex [Polish food
processing company] products reach Moscow.

Political Issues
Terrorist Killed in Hijack of Tour Bus Outside
Kremlin

ITAR-TASS Gives Account of Hijacking

LD1510081795 Moscow ITAR-TASS in English
0633 GMT 15 Oct 95

(FBIS Transcribed Text) MOSCOW October 15 (ITAR-
TASS) — All the four South Korean tourists, who were
freed as a result of an anti-terrorist operation here last
night have spent the night at the Embassy of their
country in Moscow and are due to move to a hotel
on Sunday morning. They declined any comments, Lee
Sok-bae, Press Attache of the South Korean Embassy
in Moscow, has told ITAR-TASS. Both Russians — the
bus driver and an interpreter are also having a rest.

The South Korean diplomat commended the high pro-
fessionalism of Russian commandos who stormed the
bus. "The entire operation took not more than one
minute. The terrorist fired several shots from a pistol.
But, luckily, they were not aimed. None of the hostages
was hurt. They all are safe and sound, although morally
fatigued," the diplomat said.

The bus was hijacked on Saturday at approximately
17.30 [1430 GMT] in downtown Moscow, 150 metres
from the Kremlin residence of the President of Russia
on Saturday.

An unidentified armed gunman forced his way into
the bus which carried 25 South Korean tourists and
two Russians — a driver and a tour guide, and, upon
declaring all of them to be hostages, demanded a ransom
of 30 million U.S. dollars and an unimpeded ride to one

of Moscow airports.

During the ensuing talks, in which Moscow Mayor
Yuriy Luzhkov and Federal Security Service Director
Mikhail Barsukov took most active part, the atmosphere
was considerably eased. The terrorist agreed to free all
women and then some of the men and reduced the
demanded ransom to one million USD.

By midnight on Saturday, ambulance cars were brought
to the bus which had been blockaded for eight hours
by the commandos from among elite anti-terrorist
units OMON [Special Purpose Militia Detachment) and
SOBR, a special rapid-reaction unit under the authority
of Moscow Main Police Department.

At approximately one a.m. on Sunday, the terrorist
demanded that the ransom be paid to him immediately
and that the bus be freed to ride, threatening, otherwise,
to blow up the bus and kill the hostages. He was told
that the bus was lacking fuel to reach any airport and

FBIS-SOV-95-199
16 October 1995

was Offered another bus. The gunman declined it. The
Situation was getting strained with any passing minute.

At about 02.50 the Most-Bank security van rode up
to the bus. After a bag (with money, as journalists
believe) was handed over to the terrorist, a powerful
blast occurred near the bus.

That was the beginning of a storming operation, during
which the terrorist was killed. None of the hostages
and Alpha group commandos were hurt, although the
terrorist managed to fire several pistol shots.

Moscow mayor Yuriy Luzhkov, who was at the site
of the drama, confirmed to Tass that nobody among
the hostages and the commandos had "received a single
scratch". Luzhkov said the nationality of the terrorist had
not been identified so far, but that he spoke "a southern
Russian accent”.

The Vasilyevsky spusk (bank slope) became world-
known when German pilot Mathias Rust landed his
Cessna sport plane there on May 28, 1987, in the
perestroika times of former Soviet president Mikhail
Gorbachev.

Rust succeeded to illegally cross the Soviet border and
escape from the Air Force. However, he did not escape a
Soviet jail, but was soon amnestied and returned home.

same reason. But I want to say that as a result of

following a meeting with President Yeltsin the day be-

decree on the issue was handed over to Yeltsin.

him on progress in the implementation of the federal
program Russian Farmer, which has been financed a
mere 26.1%. Fundamental measures were discussed to
settle social problems in rural areas.

Zaveryukha disagreed that agrarian reform in Russia
had stalled and added that it should not be regarded
only as the “wholesale buy-out of land from moneyless
farmers.”

16 October 1995

budget was not adopted. Presidential adviser Aleksandr

in its first reading and then passed in full by the current

Vladimir Panskov states: “There are no substantial

income was already too high and that expenditures
should be increased. He added that he had spoken to
the Prosecutor’s Office about investigating nonpayment
of taxes and that the procuracy would work with the

budget.

Asked about expenditures on Chechnya, Panskov states:
"This is the situation in Chechnya: We are not increasing
military expenditures. War is being waged within the
limits of the resources we give the Ministry of Defense
and Ministry of Internal Affairs. Of course, this has an
effect because food rations have been increased there,
money allowances have been increased, so there is an
increase in the money that was envisaged for normal,
peaceful conditions. There is a debt in paying money
allowances in the Army and in the internal affairs
bodies. This problem exists. We are resolving it, trying
to resolve it somehow. Of course, Chechnya has eaten
up money, eaten up part of that money.”

Asked whether there was any money in reserve next
year for "a new Chechnya," Panskov replies: "No, there
cannot be. Are we supposed to plan a new outbreak
of war in the budget? No, of course not, although the
budget envisages 2,000 billion rubles for restoration of
the social sphere in Chechnya.”

in politics and fail to be passed. Panskov replies that the
Finance Ministry has worked without a budget for most
of 1993. "It is easier for me as finance minister not to
have a budget," he states, and adds that, of course, he
was concerned that there should be order in the country
and therefore a budget.

Panskov concludes: “If someone accuses the budget
of being a Our Home is Russia budget, then how
will this be seen, since we propose increasing specific
expenditures on the social sphere in 1996, or on
investment in order to build our home?"
