Livshits Sees Draft Budget as Basis for Compromise

MK1410131495 Moscow SEGODNYA in Russian
14 Oct 95 p1

{Report by Aleksandr Bekker: “Aleksandr Livshits:
Budget Is Not a Dogma, But a Basis for Compromise.
Government Has Room for Maneuver"}

{[FBIS Translated Text] At SEGODNYA’s request,
Aleksandr Livshits [presidential assistant for econom-
ics] has commented on Boris Yeltsin’s statement on the
need to correct the 1996 draft budget. According to the
presidential aide, the words of the head of state must
be regarded as "a search for compromise with the State
Duma.” This is in line with all the president has said on
making the budget more socially oriented. "No good is
a government that has the only impeccable version of
the budget, leaving no room for discussion," said Mr.
Livshits.

At the same time, he believes that it would have been
more logical for the lower chamber to pass the draft
budget on its first reading. The alternative, in the aide’s
view, is tantamount to voicing no confidence in the
cabinet of ministers. The deputies’ decision to turn the
budget down on its first reading and hand it over to
a conciliation committee “is somewhat inconsistent."
Emphasizing the importance of receiving from the IMF
the $3 billion EFF [extended fund facility] loan, Mr.
Livshits let it be known that, under certain conditions,
alternative sources of covering the budget deficit might
be sought.

The presidential aide said that a revision of the social
and economic development forecast and a recalculation
of the budget parameters could be made in a civilized
way without violating the budget’s basic guidelines.
"If monthly inflation is assumed at, for instance, 1.4
percent instead of the currently assumed 1.2 percent,
this, I think, does not exceed a reasonable level of
compromise,” said Aleksandr Livshits.

Comptroller’s Office Questions Budget Assumptions

MMI610092495 Moscow IZVESTIYA (Financial
Section) in Russian 13 Oct 95 pl

[Report by Yevgeniya Pismennaya: "Comptroller's Of-
fice Negative About 1996 Budget")

{(FBIS Translated Text] The Russian Comptroller’s
Office has submitted its conclusion on the draft budget
for 1996.

The Comptroller’s Office specialists claim that the
strategy of financial stabilization based on minimizing
inflation and the budget deficit and placing tough limits
on the expenditure side of the budget is not being

This report may contain copyrighted material. Copying and dissemination
is prohibited without permission of the copyright owners.

64 RUSSIA NATIONAL AFFAIRS

justified. In reality, many of the main provisions of the
president’s message on budgetary policy in 1996 are
not being implemented. The budget’s revenue indicators
are not based on any specific measures or decisions. The
draft budget for 1996 almost wholly maintains the thrust
of 1995 budgetary policy and fails to ensure financial
stabilization.

The budget is based on unrealistic initial conditions
when it comes to the inflation rate. This year inflation
is already significantly in excess of the government’s
forecasts in the 1995 budget. The government estimates
that consumer price growth in the current year will be
283-294 percent — or 6.3-7 percent a month — with
inflation falling to 2-3 percent in December. Consumer
prices have already increased by 100-140 percent in
January-July this year, matching the increase last year.
Total monetary resources as of 1 January this year came
to 97,825.9 billion rubles [R], and had increased by 1
July to R156,732.9 billion — or 160.2 percent. This
means that we can predict that monthly inflation will be
6-8 percent this fall and around 10 percent by the new
year. According to the Comptroller’s Office predictions,
actual inflation this year will be approximately threefold,
as opposed to the government estimate of 110-130

percent.

The first (optimistic) draft of the budget for 1996 is
based on achieving inflation of 2-2.5 percent a month
by the end of 1995 and keeping inflation to 1 percent
a month throughout 1996 (GDP deflator 135, consumer
price growth 133). Under the second (pessimistic) draft,
inflation is reduced to 3.5 percent a month by the end
of the year and kept at 3 percent a month during 1996
(deflator 160, consumer price growth 160). According
to government estimates, the deflator will fall next
year by 1.96 times under the first draft and by 1.66
times under the second. This indicator stood at 1.69 in
1995 compared with 1994. Taking account of the actual
inflation indicators expected this year, the deflator will
fall in 1996 by 2.67 times (version one), or by 2.26 times
(version two), and by 1.25 times this year compared
with last year.

There are also doubts about the GDP estimates. The
government estimates that the anticipated execution of
the 1995 budget should come to R213.76 trillion for
revenue, corresponding to 13.53 percent of expected
GDP (the ratified budget gives a figure of 18.8 per-
cent) and R272.51 trillion for expenditure (17.2 percent
of anticipated GDP against a set figure of 26.7 per-
cent). The deficit is R83.266 trillion for revenue and
R150.1 trillion for expenditure. If the actual anticipated
level of inflation (approximately threefold) and the ac-
celerated growth of the GDP deflator (3.61) are taken
into account, we obtain a larger anticipated level of

FBIS-SOV-95-199
16 October 1995

GDP (approximately R2,152.38 trillion), revenue short-
fall (R113.43 trillion), and expenditure underfinancing
(R204.48 trillion). This is what has caused the state’s
failure to meet its commitments, and hence the chain
reaction of defaulting.

The government estimate of R1,580 trillion for GDP
in 1995 has been used as the basis for the 1996 GDP
estimate of R2,100 trillion, even though it seems more
realistic to assume that the level will be at least R2,150
trillion. Consequently, next year’s budget needs, even
on the basis of the inflation levels used, to apply a
GDP figure of at least R2,900 trillion under option one
and R3,400 trillion under option two. Given that in
the context of a continuing production slump and the
adopted budget and fiscal strategy inflation is unlikely
to fall more than 1.2-1.5 times, and the deflator should
at least be no less than inflation, we can expect GDP to
be at least R3,750-4,250 trillion.

It is obvious that the revenue and expenditure sides of
the budget need reworking. Otherwise, the Comptrol-
ler’s Office predicts, this year’s situation, which has
produced large amounts of defaulting that are the state’s
fault, will be further compounded. "Disguising" the bud-
get deficit, as has been done in the past, will not resolve
the situation, but will make it intractable.

Moreover, there are many contradictory indicators in the
draft budget regarding the reform programs for 1995-
1997. The task is set of reviving economic growth but,
at the same time, there is a forecast of a decline in
domestic demand, a further deterioration in the structure
of fixed capital, an ongoing outflow of capital from
the production sphere to the sphere of financial and
brokerage activity, a decline in investment activity, and
a continuing tendency for the economy to be geared to
raw materials.

The forecast presupposes a further decline in pay as a
proportion of GDP, the stagnation of the population's
monetary income, an increase of 1.5 million in the num-
ber of registered unemployed, and a rate of unemploy-
ment hitting 4.2-4.8 percent. This does not square with
the stated social thrust of President Yeltsin’s economic

policy.

The explanatory note to the draft federal budget for 1996
claims that the social sphere and support for investment
have been given priority in budgetary policy for the
coming year. It follows from the consolidated financial
balance sheet that this expenditure as a proportion of
the budget will remain at the current year’s level (9.5
percent in 1994, 5.6 percent in 1995, approximately 5.8
percent in 1996).

This report may contain copyrighted material. Copying and dissemination
is prohibited without permission of the copyright owners.

FBIS-SOV-95-199
16 October 1995

The Comptroller’s Office claims that incorrect compar-
isons of items of expenditure in the ratified budget as
a percentage of GDP have been used in assessing this
year’s results. In particular, the comparison of items of
budget expenditure has been done on the basis that GDP
in 1995 will be R1,580 trillion rather than the R930 tril-
lion that was used as the basis for the main federal bud-
get indicators this year. This leads to the creation of the
impression that the proportion of expenditure in 1996
GDP has increased. The explanatory note provides ex-
penditure as a proportion of GDP for 20 main categories,
revealing growth for all but two. The Comptroller’s Of-
fice calculations show that spending is actually falling
this year. And it is this expenditure that is being used
as the basis for calculating proportions for all categories
of spending in 1996.

Chernomyrdin, Vasilyev Cited on Economy,
Inflation

LD1210154195 Moscow ITAR-TASS in English
1532 GMT 12 Oct 95

(By Gennady Yezhov]

{[FBIS Transcribed Text} MOSCOW, October 12
(ITAR-TASS) — Russian Prime Minister Viktor Cher-
nomyrdin asked today’s meeting of the government to
declare unsatisfactory the government’s efforts, exerted
in the first ten months of this year, to implement Presi-
dent Yeltsin’s 1995 message to the Russian parliament.
The cabinet was considering the implementation of
the comprehensive plan for the government’s actions
to implement the message, and also the government
programme to further the reforms and develop the
Russian economy in 1995-1997. Both the plan and the
programme envisage concrete efforts to create a solid
normative-legal basis in the country, which will ensure
the success of economic reforms in Russia. In spite of
this, Chernomyrdin said, the country now has only 50
per cent of the needed legislations.

Deputy Minister for Economics Sergey Vasilyev, who
reported io the government on this problem, said that
the plan envisaged the elaboration, in the period from
June to September, of 58 per cent of the needed norma-
tive documents, pertaining to the financial, monetary-
crediting, investment, structural policies, to the insti-
tutional reforms, including the agricultural and social
policies, the housing reform, the foreign economic and
regional policies, the protection of the environment and
the use of natural resources. More than twenty min-
istries and other agencies are taking part in the drafting
of these bills.

However, only ten normative documents had been
approved by September 25, 1995, and seven other

RUSSIA NATIONAL AFFAIRS 65

bills had been submitted for approval to the State
Duma and the President. Moreover, seventeen draft
normative documents are now being debated within the
government, readied for signing or approval by the State
Duma. Twenty others are in the making or are being
thrashed out with the agencies concerned.

Chernomyrdin instructed his First Deputy Anatoly
Chubays to check the corresponding work of ministries
and other agencies and to submit to the next meeting of
the government a document on the the extend to which
the comprehensive plan for the cabinet’s actions and the
government's programme to implement the president's
message have been fulfilled. “Those guilty will be
punished if their work is found to be unsatisfactory, up
to and including ministers," Chernomyrdin stressed.

Moreover, the premier promised that the progress of
the implementation of the president’s message would
henceforth be discussed at each meeting of the govern-
ment. It was also moved to alter the method and proce-
dure for drawing up bills. It often happens that a federal
bill or a federal programme, approved by the govern-
ment, is backed up only by 20 per cent of the needed
financial means. Chernomyrdin stressed that it was im-
permissible to allow "a Finance Ministry official" adopt
decisions on the allocation of money to finance this or
that federal programme. "It is the government that will
adopt such decisions, and it will decide what and to
whom should be allocated each year. The government
must primarily honour its own decisions. It must guar-
antee all the financial means needed by the programmes
it approves,” the premier stressed.

Dwelling on some other key directions of the implemen-
tation of the President’s message, Vasilyev noted that
a steady drop of monthly inflation rates was observed
throughout the year due to the taken measures, although
it was impossible to cut down inflation to 1-2 per cent
a month by the second half of 1995, as it stipulated by
the government’s programme. For instance, it equalied
to 4.6 per cent in August, which is far below the av-
erage for 1994 (10-12 per cent a month). Nevertheless,
Vasilyev pointed out, the danger of a lasting depression
was not done away with. In this situation, investments
in production can be expected to perk up not earlier
than 1998-1999. The main reasons behind this year’s
growing inflation, Vasilyev said, include such factors
as the creation of the Central Bank’s currency reserves,
which made for the growth of rouble supply over and
above the envisaged volumes, the government's insuf-
ficient control over the activities of natural monopolies,
and the heightened inflationary expectations of the en-
terprises and population.

Premier Highlights Positive Developments in
Economy

MMI1610094095 Moscow ROSSIYSKIYE VESTI
in Russian 14 Oct 95 p 3

(Unattributed report: "Viktor Chernomyrdin: Preserving
the Pace of the Positive Changes"]

(FBIS Translated Text] The scale of the fall in social
production has lessened compared with the same period
last year, Viktor Chernomyrdin said Friday {13 October]
at the State Duma when delivering a report on the
socioeconomic situation of the Russian Federation. GDP
fell by just 3 percent in the first nine months of the year,
one-fourth of last year’s figure. Production growth has
been noted in a number of sectors, although every sector
has enterprises that are "prostrate." The economy has
begun not only to survive, according to the premier,
but has also begun to develop in line with market laws.
The introduction of currency banding has had positive
results; it has thereby proved possible to reduce the
degree of dollarization of the economy and stabilize the
tuble system.

The government chairman also pointed to negative
e/ements. These include the 12-percent reduction in the
population’s real incomes whereby 40 million people
have ended up with less than the minimum subsistence
level. This is the result of the failure to secure the
planned level of inflation. For the same reason it has

FBIS-SOV-95-199
16 October 1995

not proved possible to change the situation with regard
to investments, the influx of which is lagging behind
the anticipated level. Nonpayments are affecting the
economy severely.

Speaking about the formation of a banking system,
Chernomyrdin stressed that the August crisis marks the
Crisis stage in an illness after which recovery begins. In
his view we are on the verge of establishing the coun-
try’s banking system, in whose stabilization the state
securities market should play a major role. The premier
familiarized deputies with the steps the government is
proposing to take to consolidate the steady positive eco-
nomic trends. It is planned to introduce the mechanism
of state regulation of the natural monopolies, including
the power industry, railroad transport, and the oil and
gas industry. Federal commissions will be set up which
should, inter alia, ensure that the rise in the price of the
natural monopolies’ output is no more than 70 percent
of inflation. The government also intends to carry on
providing support for the base sectors of industry —
coal, metallurgy, etc. The 1996 state budget must en-
visage a state order for defense complex enterprises. In
the near future the government will examine a program
for the development of agriculture in which it will em-
ploy new forms of cooperation with the agrarian sector
capable of ensuring more effective use and repayment
of credit.

Speaking about land, Chernomyrdin noted that it should
belong to an owner, to someone who is able and
willing to work effectively on that land. It must be both
inherited and pass from a bad owner to a good owner.

The premier dwelt especially on the problem of duped
investors. He condemned the wrongful practice of
creating financial pyramids and said that there are
plans to set up a compensation fund which will pledge
to restore citizens’ money. Steps will also be taken
to galvanize law-enforcement bodies in suppressing
the activity of organizations that resemble financial
pyramids.

Among the government's top priorities the premier
listed the elimination of wage arrears and the resolution
of the issue of pension indexation. He touched on the
Situation in the coal industry, noting that the state has
paid the miners’ wage arrears and that their problems are
because of the insolvency of associated sectors and their
customers. Mutual offsetting of debts will be employed
in the near future to resolve the crisis. He also urged
the heads of local administrations to take steps to curb

Gathering, Processing of Income Statistics Viewed

MM1310153795 Moscow TRUD in Russian
13 Oct 95 pS

[Article by political observer Vitaliy Golovachev: “State
Knows Perfectly Well What We Have in Our Pockets.
At the Request of Statisticians 49,000 Russians Have
Kept Household ‘Ledgers,’ Scrupulously Recording
Income and Expenditure Every Day. This Information
Is Kept Strictly Secret")

(FBIS Translated Text] For many decades the State
Committee for Statistics has been gathering and sum-
marizing every month information about the income and
expenditure of families of various means in almost all
“typical” groups and strata of the population, as spe-

RUSSIA NATIONAL AFFAIRS 67

cialists say, and in all regions throughout the country.
In the former USSR, 90,000 family budgets of blue- and
white-collar workers, kolkhoz workers, and pensioners
were analyzed. (The incomes of as many as 310,000
families were studied during one-shot selective investi-
gations). But until 1989 all this material was kept com-
pletely secret. A total of 49,000 family budgets aze used
as a “base” in the Russian Federation.

This information is of extreme importance. Because on
its basis (following the appropriate computer process-
ing, summarization, and systematization) the authorities
assess processes going on in the social sphere, changes
in the population’s living standard, and the scale of
poverty, and it must be assumed, they then adopt fun-
damental political and economic decisions. That is why
this information should be comprehensive and exact.

In each of the 49,000 families, one of the family
members keeps detailed daily records of all income and
expenditure. Usually it is the housewife — women are
more punctilious, patient, and, most importantly they
do the shopping and run the household. At the same
time, statistical services officials guarantee complete
anonymity and promise to keep all the information
obtained a strict secret. The budgets of the same
families are examined throughout the year. In many
cases such observations are conducted for several years
running. Curiously, when they come to an end, the
home accountants, who have gotten used to scrupulously
recording all income and expenditure, often continue to
do so.

But perhaps the most difficult question is how accurate
is the information obtained by the statistical services
about the rich and poor. As for those who are poorly
provided for, there the information is very reliable, so
specialists believe — “the destitute have nothing to
hide.” The task is more difficult with those whose living
conditions are better. In Soviet times, for example,
“operators” did not get selected. And now that earnings
constitute less than one-half of personal income, there
are not all that many people willing to talk about
their earnings on the side (frequently concealed from
tax inspectors) and other income. It is very difficult,
frequently impossible, to obtain information about the
very rich — bankers, entrepreneurs, “the new Russians."
The same situation prevails in all countries throughout
the world, incidentally.

What has the examination of family budgets in the past
five years revealed? In fact, something of which we are
all already well aware: a rapid increase in the extent of
poverty. Whereas in 1990 there were just over 2 million
Russians below the poverty line (taking into account the
minimum set of products, goods, and services necessary

This report may contain copyrighted material. Copying and dissemination
is prohibited without permission of the copyright owners.

68 RUSSIA NATIONAL AFFAIRS

for survival), then in 1992, following the beginning of
the radical reforms, the figure was 50 million (one-
third of the country), reaching 47 million in 1993, 45
million in the first quarter of this year, and 35 million
in August....

The stratification of society is increasing sharply. In
1991 the income of the 15 million least well-provided
for Russians was exceeded by the income of the
15 million best-provided for Russians by a factor
of 4.5. Last year it was a factor of 15. The most
dangerous phenomenon is stagnant poverty. When a
family temporarily finds itself in a difficult situation (a
young couple with children or someone who temporarily
becomes unable to work) then this can be overcome, and
people do not lose hope. But stagnant poverty generates
irreversible changes in way of life and psychology,
which results in spiritual traumas that are hard to cure.
The examination of family budgets has shown that last
year, for 46 percent of the population living below the
poverty line, the poverty was temporary, while for 54
percent (around 20 million Russians) it was stagnant
and constant over the year.

Statistics also have the answer to the following question:
How much money do the poor need for their income to
reach the subsistence wage? A vast sum — 23.3 trillion
rubles [R] for January-August of this year.

The average money income of the 30 million Russians
with the greatest income was R1.085 million rubles,
whereas it did not exceed R147,000 for the 30 million
poorest citizens. The fight against poverty has become
one of the most important problems in Russia today.

In the next few months the State Committee for Statis-
tics is planning to substantially change and expand the
circle of Russians polled. Specialists will visit people’s
apartments, and maybe they will knock on your door,
dear reader. Do not refuse to let them in. First of all,
complete confidentiality is guaranteed. Second, it is not
just the leaders, but all of us who really must know how
the Russians live...

Lack of Fuel May Cause Powerplants To Cut
Supplies

MK1410062595 Moscow SEGODNYA in Russian
12 Oct 95 p4

(Unattributed report: "Electric Power Workers Warn
That Electricity Supply May Be Cut")

(FBIS Translated Text] Russian power generation facili-
ties are “on the verge of a standstill" because of virtually

FBIS-SOV-95-199
16 October 1995

"zero fuel reserves" — a situation that gives rise to a
fully “realistic threat" that certain oblasts and regions of
the Russian Federation may be cut off from thermal and
electric energy sources, Viktor Kudryavatyy, first vice
president of the “Russian Single Electric Power Sys-
tem" joint-stock company [RSEPS], said at a meeting
held at the Russian Federation Fuel and Energy Min-
istry on Wednesday [11 October]. In his words, a par-
ticularly worrying situation is coming about in the Cen-
tral Region of the Russian Federation, where 70 percent
of energy-generating facilities are gas-operated. During
the first 9 months of 1995, Mr. Kudryavatyy said, the
"Gazprom" joint-stock company delivered to the electric
power industry 6 billion cubic meters of gas less than
during the same period of 1994. At present, gas deliv-
eries to Russia’s largest Konakovskaya, Kostromskaya,
Permskaya, Pechorskaya, and Ryazanskaya State Re-
gional Electric Power Stations have been halved. "Any
further halt in gas supplies to these stations will force
power industry workers to cut electric and thermal en-
ergy supplies to all customers regardless of whether or
not they pay their energy bills," Mr. Kudryavatyy em-
phasized.

RSEPS specialists blame the electric power stations’
fuel predicament on a lack of money needed to purchase
fuel. As of 1 October, the debts of electricity consumers
have already exceeded 27 trillion rubles. At the same
time, as was noted at the meeting, which was attended
by "Gazprom" officials, the electric power industry’s
debt for gas reached nearly 8.4 trillion rubles by 1
October. On the whole, 58 percent of Russia’s thermal
electric power stations are gas-operated.

NATO Delegation Meets Ukrainian Officials in Kiev

were not discussed at a briefing in the Foreign Affairs
Ministry of Ukraine on 13 October, Volodymyr Khan-
dohiy, deputy foreign minister of Ukraine, said after the
briefing, which was held for the officials and experts of
Ukraine’s ministries and departments and attended by a
NATO delegation, led by Gebhardt von Moltke, assis-
tant secretary general for political affairs. According to
Khandohiy, the Ukrainian side was informed about the
criteria and conditions for new countries’ admission to
the alliance; in particular, about the future structure of
NATO forces, "including the nuclear complex."

Khandohiy said the issue of possible deployment of
nuclear weapons on the territories of NATO’s new
member states had not been discussed either. He said it
was a hypothetical issue for the moment. However, the
conversation with the NATO delegation made it clear
that “nothing is ruled out when a country becomes a
NATO member state."

Replying to journalists’ questions, Gebhardt von Moltke
said that “research on NATO's expansion" “outlines
the trends of Ukraine’s participation with regard to
its contribution to the European security." He said
NATO was not making any preliminary demands on
the deployment of nuclear weapons on the territories of
NATO’s new member states.

The assistant secretary general said NATO was not
postponing the issue of admitting new members until
1997.

Gebhardt von Moltke pointed tc the importance of
consolidating security in cooperation with Russia, in
order to prevent Europe from being divided into blocs.

EBRD Proposes Alternatives to Chernobyl Power
Plant

AU1310151795 Duesseldorf HANDELSBLATT
in German 12 Oct 95 p8

[Report by "jow": "Kiev to Reorganize Energy Supply)

(FBIS Translated Text] Kiev, 11 October — The future
of the Chernobyl! nuclear power plant continues to be
a controversial issue between Ukraine and its partners
in the West. Ukraine points out that it cannot do with-
out the energy produced in Chernobyl! without receiving
appropriate compensation. A German government del-
egation is currently trying to convince the government
of the CIS republic of the necessity of closing down
the plant. Germany seems to be prepared to provide ad-
ditional means for technical assistance to achieve this
aim. The German delegation is planning to meet with
President Leonid Kuchma and Prime Miiiister Yevheniy
Marchuk.

A study by the European Bank for Reconstruction
and Development (EBRD) has become known in Kiev,
which suggests that the closure of the Chernobyl nuclear
power plant could be compensated for by moderniz-
ing the existing Ukrainian power plants. The study thus
agrees with the proposals of Siemens AG on moderniz-
ing the existing coal power plants, rather than the plans
of competitor ABB Asea Brown Boveri AG, which
wants to establish a new combined gas plant near Cher-
noby!.

In an interview with HANDELSBLATT, the head of
the EBRD office in Kiev, Yaroslav Kinach, said that he
considers the Siemens concept "much better, econom-
ical, and logical" than the proposals by ABB. "Why
should Ukraine build another colossus that the country
cannot afford," Kinach said. The ABB suggestion of a
gas power plant would also worsen Ukraine’s foreign
trade balance, since additional natural gas would have
to be imported from Russia. Kinach does not like the

FBIS-SOV-95-199
16 October 1995

idea of a nuclear alternative to Chernobyl either: “Fol-
lowing the problems in the Slovak Republic, the EBRD
is not interested in financing nuclear power plants in
Ukraine.”

The EBRD is playing an important role as mediator in
the talks between Ukraine and its partner states. Also,
the seven leading industrial states (G-7) and the OECD
have a special account with the EBRD for nuclear safety
in eastern Europe.

According to Kinach, the EBRD has commissioned
experts to examine all power plants in Ukraine as to
their condition and development potential. The outcome
shows that Ukraine has sufficient energy potential to
implement a “minimum cost alternative:" Investment in
the modernization of coal power plants, hydroelectric
plants, and district heating plants on the supply side,
and energy consumption programs on the users’ side.
This program could strongly reduce companies’ energy
consumption and would make energy consumption
measurable direct at the consumers’ end, thus making
them more aware of it. "People have to see what
they are using and pay for it," Kinach said. At least
partial privatization and opening it to foreign investors
would certainly be conducive to a reform of the energy
industry.

Kinach says that beside Siemens AG Canadian firms
have made proposals for modernizing the conventional
energy industry. Private Canadian energy producer
Northland Power Ltd. thus wants to invest in a modern
district heating plant near Kiev. The United States also
sees some potential in hydroelectric power: Ukrainian
Prime Minister Yevheniy Marchuk has brought a new
credit line of the U.S. Eximbank back from his visit to
the United States for modernizing hydroelectric power
plants.

Chernoby! Closure Discussed With G-7 Experts

LD1310151295 Kiev UNIAN in Ukrainian
1850 GMT 13 Oct 95

[FBIS Translated Text) Kiev, 12 Oct — Experts from
Ukraine and the G-7 countries have ended a round
of talks on the closure of Chernobyl nuclear power
plant, the plant’s representatives told UNIAN. The two
sides discussed specific projects to be included into an
agreement between Ukraine and the G-7 on cooperation
in the decommissioning of Chernobyl power units.

The main project considered was the G-7 proposal to
compensate the loss of Chernobyl’s capacity by mod-
ernizing thermal power stations. Ukraine’s earlier pro-

This report may contain copyrighted material. Copying and disseminaticn
is prohibited without permission of the copyright owners.

FBIS-SOV-95-199
16 October 1995

the ways of reforming thermal power engineering in
Ukraine.

Ukrainian representatives proposed a four-stage process
of decommissioning the Chernobyl nuclear power sta-
tion. The first stage, to be completed by the year 2000,
would include project preparation, the construction of a
new nuclear waste repository and the establishment of
a centre for nuclear waste disposal. In the second stage,
until 2003, power units would be phased out and nu-
clear fuel removed. The third stage would cover the de-
contamination of equipment, by 2004, while the fourth
would deal with the conservation of power units, by
2006. All power units would be completely nuclear-safe
by 2016. Following this schedule, Ukraine proposed a
joint action plan to fund the projects.

The results of the talks will be summed up after a
meeting with G-7 representatives at the political level
in early November, Nur Nihmatulin, deputy chairman
of the State Committee for the Use of Nuclear Power,
said.

LD1310165995 Moscow INTERFAX in English

Economic Issues

Marchuk Calls For ‘Serious’ Foreign Investment

LD1310195495 Moscow INTERFAX in English
1741 GMT 13 Oct 95

[From the “Presidential Bulletin" feature: compiled by
Nikolay Zherebtsov, Andrey Petrovskiy, and edited by
Vladimir Shishlin}

([FBIS Transcribed Text] The government is interested
in attracting foreign investment needed for the country’s
economic recovery, Prime Minister Yevheniy Marchuk
believes.

Speaking in Kiev on Thursday, he said nothing short of
serious foreign investment could help Ukraine get out
of crisis.

Marchuk pledged commitment to cooperation with in-
ternational financiers but not at the cost of the nation’s
wealth.

Although the country’s foreign debt runs at $8.8 billion,
the prime minister is sure that Ukraine needs further tens
of billions of dollars to reform its energy sector, create
a full cycle of nuclear fuel production and changes to
its agro-industrial complex.

On G-7 talks dealing with the question of a possible
closure of the Chernobyl nuclear power plant, Marchuk
spoke of signs of progress after a setback caused
by uncertainty in Kiev’s position before. He said the
government had now formed a uniform Chernoby!
policy.

Although Marchuk said the government knew of how
much G-7 states were prepared to allocate to get
Chernoby! shut down, he did not elaborate.

IF Note: In the meantime, general director of the
Chernobyl plant Sergey Parashin said that G-7 must
send a higher level delegation to decide on the question
of the plant closure.

This report may contain copyrighted material. Copying and dissemination
is prohibited without permission of the copyright owners.

74 UKRAINE

Parashin spoke of having met US Energy Secretary
Hazel O’Leary during a recent visit to Washington to
attend the world economic congress who promised to
ask President Bill Clinton for greater US involvement
in resolving the Chernoby! issue.

On gas imports from Russia in 1996, Marchuk expressed
the hope that an appropriate agreement would be signed.

He said negotiation was under way but it was very hard.

Marchuk said that a presidential decree would be
issued soon on government subsidies to household gas
consumers, but the industries would have to import
gas under direct contracts with Russia’s Gazprom and
Turkmenistan.

He said professional go-betweens would be appointed
by the government to ensure such supplies.

On the whole, Marchuk said, Ukraine will continue to
depend on gas imports from Russia in the next few
years though Kiev was looking for alternative sources
of energy imports.

Cabinet Criticizes Parliament’s Low-Income
Threshold

LD1310214895 Kiev UT-1 Television Network
in Ukrainian 1900 GMT 13 Oct 95

(FBIS Translated Text] Today we received the text of
a statement by the Cabinet of Ministers of Ukraine. It
says:

On 12 October 1995, the Supreme Council of Ukraine
passed a resolution establishing a new level of the low-
income threshold. Unfortunately, the attempt to take this
decision is nothing else but a purely populist move.
Its implementation will definitely lead to disastrous
consequences in the economy and a further decline in
the living standards of the population. The thing is that
there exist no sources to provide for the implementation
of the resointion other than those leading to higher
inflation and additional emissi~:

According to calculations made, a minimum increase
in payments from the budget alone will require an ad-
ditional 230 trillion karbovantsy per month in order to
implement the resolution. Meanwhile, the budget rev-
enue in August amounted to as little as approximately
220 trillion karbovantsy. In such a situation, the overall
monthly budget spending will exceed the budget rev-
enue by 150 percent. The existing funds are enough to
pay just one in every three citizens who receive wages,
pensions, or budget allowances. The above decision can
only be implemented at the expense of the emission by
the end of the year [1995] to the value of 590 trillion
karbovantsy, which exceeds by 160 percent the volume

FBIS-SOV-95-199
16 October 1995

of the emission over the last nine months. Moreover, by
the end of the year [1995], consumer prices will grow
by 180 percent, or approximately 40 percent per month
on average. As early as November, real incomes of the
population will decrease by 20 percent. People with low
incomes will be affected most of all, because prices for
the vital consumer goods will be growing at a higher
rate.

The situation at self-supporting enterprises will also
be sharply aggravated. At present, they owe about 30
trillion karbovantsy to their employees. Meanwhile, the
establishment of the minimum wage at a level of 4.8
billion karbovantsy will lead to a significant growth in
prices for their products or in a dismissal of about 40
percent of employees. There will be another recession,
the financial situation of enterprises and the payments
crisis will be sharply aggravated. These actions by
the Supreme Council will not contribute to the social
protection of our compatriots in real terms; moreover,
they will lead to their further impoverishment, upset
the economic balance, trigger another wave of inflation,
undermine the karbovanets exchange rate and bring the
country into a very difficult situation at a time when
preparations for the winter season are under way.

The above negative phenomena are not just experts’
forecast — unfortunately, the negative experience of
the past also proves this. Populism should not be
allowed to inflict new losses on people. One can hardly
imagine that the Supreme Council leadership — which
submitted the issue for the consideration of parliament
without coordinating it with the Cabinet of Ministers
and without taking into consideration the government's
position during the debate — is unaware of this. The
decision runs counter to the government's program
approved by parliament the day before and to the law
of Ukraine on the state budget of Ukraine for 1995.

The government declares that it will pursue unswerv-
ingly the poiicy of social support to the population and
take measures toward that end outlined in the govern-
ment’s program, which was approved by parliament.

For example, since 1 October this year, the government
has increased by 22 percent pensions, grants, social
benefits, and wages in the budget-supported sphere. It is
envisaged to grant one-off aid to some categories of the
population to buy fuel and meet other needs connected
with preparations for the winter season. The government
believes that the resolution by the Supreme Council of
Ukraine on the low-income threshold is economically
unfounded and impossible to implement. Therefore, the
Cabinet of Ministers appeals to the Supreme Council
and to the people’s deputies of Ukraine to revise the
issue, with government representatives taking part in

16 October 1995

the debate on a compulsory basis, in order to prevent
another economic shock, and to find, in cooperation with
the Cabinet of Ministers, realistic ways of providing
social support to the population of Ukraine.

Parliament Sets Higher Poverty Level

WS1310114795 Kiev INTELNEWS in English
0139 GMT 13 Oct 95

{Report by Oksana Hasiuk]

(FBIS Transcribed Text] Kiev, Oct. 12 — Parliament
Thursday [12 October] set a new poverty level for
Ukrainians, at KBV4.8 [karbovantski] million, or about
US$ 27 a month. The former level was less than half
that amount, or KBV2.1 million.

The national legislature ordered the government to carry
all the costs of the new poverty level without raising the
budget deficit or borrowing money.

The government has to figure out how to do this by Nov.
1, or the resolution will go into effect automatically
and raise pensions and stipends of millions of Ukrainian
poor people.

Yuriy Buzduhan, who heads the Social Protection
Committee in Parliament, said that the real poverty level
should be nearer to KBV7 million, or about US$ 40.

He said his committee found that "the average Ukrainian
family spends at least 60 percent of its money on food.”

The Parliament resolution would also raise Ukraine's
minimum wage, which is the lowest in the world, to
meet the new poverty level.

Labor Minister Mykhailo Kaskevich said the “Thursday
decision will cause prices to double or triple in Decem-
ber” because the government lacks the money to pay it.

The labor minister also said that the Parliament vote
would practically destroy the government program the
Rada [parliament] passed Wednesday, because of the
high spending it would cause.

Payments Crisis Said Not Caused by Assets
Shortage

LD1510114095 Kiev UNIAN in Ukrainian
1520 GMT 15 Oct 95

(FBIS Translated Text] Kiev, 14 Oct (UNIAN) — Vik-
tor Lysytskyy, macroeconomic adviser to the chairman
of the board of the National Bank of Ukraine told
UNIAN: "One should not believe that the payments cri-
sis in Ukraine has been caused by the shortage of enter-
prises’ circulating assets. The National Bank has made
calculations which prove that Ukraine's enterprises have
enough funds to settle accounts with their trading part-
ners.”

In the first six months of 1995, the circulating assets
of enterprises, excluding banks, grew by 2 quadi.llion
karbovantsi. The growth rate in the first six months of
1995 amounted to 228 percent and was higher than the
rate of inflation in the same period — 184.9 percent
(the National Bank estimates enterprises’ circulating
assets on a six month basis). According to the latest
calculations, enterprises made a profit of 585 trillion
0 (excluding banks, joint ventures, small businesses,
and cooperative societies). Of this sum 84 trillion
karbovantsi were spent as incentives (14.3 percent of
the profit) and 68 trillion karbovantsi were spent for
other purposes (11.7 percent of the profit).

Lysytskyy believes the money could be used to settle
enterprises’ credit indebtedness. In this case the state
budget would receive 15 trillion karbonvantsi in value-
added tax and excise duty.

In the adviser’s opinion, enterprises are overpricing
their products. He believes the allegations that Ukraine's
industry is unprofitable do not correspond to reality.

According to National Bank of Ukraine calculations,
the profitability of the food industry is 39.6 percent,
the clothes-making industry 30.7 percent, the machine-
building and metal-working industries 28.6 percent, and
the gas industry 35.2 percent.

According to Lysytskyy, enterprises should decrease
their profitability by 10 to 15 percent in order to make
prices competitive.

Belarus

Lukashenka Orders Resumed Dismantling of
Military Armor

LD1310154095 Moscow ITAR-TASS in English
1424 GMT 13 Oct 95

[By Leonid Tratsevskiy]

{FBIS Transcribed Text) MINSK, October 13 (Itar-
Tass) - The scrapping of armour, which was suspended
due to money shortage, was resumed on October 15 [as
received], after a lengthy break, at a tank-repair plant
in Borisov on decison of Belarus President Alyaksandr
Lukashenka.

An inspection team, including eight U.S. military ex-
perts, one Italian and one German representative, arrived
in the Belarus Republic today in keeping with the In-
ternational Agreement on Conventional Armaments in
Europe. The NATO military inspection will record the
beginning of the planned period of amraments reduc-
tion.

President Lukashenka, who visited the Borisov tank-
repair plant the day before, noted that “the Belarus
Republic was scrupulously fulfilling its international
disarmament commitments”. The Head of the Belarus
State said that the republic was resuming at its own
expense the costly process of scrapping tanks and
armoured personnel carriers. The President pointed
out that the destruction of armour would be done in
strict compliance with the terms of the International
Agreement on Conventional Armed Forces in Europe,
given western assistance to this effort.

U.S. Experts Check Resumption of Arms
Destruction

WS1610103595 Minsk BELAPAN in English
0805 GMT 16 Oct 95

{[FBIS Transcribed Text] On 13 October, a group of
American military experts arrived in Belarus to visit
the 140th repair works of the Ministry of Defense in
Barysaw, which resumes the destruction of military
equipment in excess of the limits specified in the
Conventional Armed Forces in Europe [CFE] Treaty.

The experts will verify the availability and readiness of
the equipment Belarus declared for destruction during
the first calendar period after the Republic resumed the
fultiliment of its commitments under the CFE Treaty.

As a BELAPAN correspondent was informed at the
Ministry of Defense press-office, this calendar period
will last until 14 December. Within these two months,
the Barysaw works is to destroy 137 tanks and 142
armored combat vehicles.

Russian military officers in Belarus, one of the heads
of the president’s control service first said that he knew
nothing. However, later he threatened to set the KGB
on the correspondent since such facts were on the whole
not only true but were considered to be secret. And this
meant, above all, that they were hiding a lot. This is
Aleksandr Stupnikov in Minsk, Belarus.

Chyhir: Economy ‘May Not Stand’ Increase in
Wages

WS1310115695 Minsk BELAPAN in English
0806 GMT 11 Oct 95

([FBIS Transcribed Text] At the present time an increase
in the minimum monthly wage rate is not expedient,
though this point has been discussed by the Cabinet
and the Presidential Administration for a long time,
Prime Minister Mikhail Chyhir said at the October 9
government press conference. According to him, this
year wages rose more quickly than prices for consumer
goods, and an average monthly wage is approaching
to the equivalent of 80 US Dollars. The economy may
not stand the attempt to increase it, for instance, up to
100- 110 dollars. “In this case we will get the galloping
inflation which was observed in the Republic not long
ago”, the Prime Minister said.

78 WESTERN REGION

Constitutional Court Regards Supreme Soviet as
Legitimate

MK1310125795 Moscow SEGODNYA in Russian
12 Oct 95 pl

{Report by Valentin Zhdanko: “The Constitutional Court
of Belarus Has Recognized the Supreme Soviet as
Legitimate")

(FBIS Translated Text) Yesterday, the Constitutional
Court of Belarus recognized the 12th convocation of
the Supreme Soviet of that country as legitimate. After
a five-hour discussion on this issue behind closed doors,
the court ruled that the Supreme Soviet should remain
the highest representative and legislative body until the
Supreme Soviet of the 13th convocation holds its first
session.

For several months now, President Alyaksandr
Lukashenka has been ruling the country by edicts that
are in obvious contradiction to many of the active laws.
In so doing, Mr. Lukashenka refers to the fact that the
Supreme Soviet has exceeded the term of its office and,
hence, the laws passed by it are not binding.

After the Constitutional Court declared one of the pres-
idential edicts — a key document dealing with adjust-
ments to the state budget — as unconstitutional, some
high-ranking officials insisted that the Constitutional
Court initiate a case of illegitimacy against the par-
liament itself as a legislative body. Had the Constitu-
tional Court agreed on the illegitimacy of the Belarusi2n
Supreme Soviet, the president could have hardly been
reproached for issuing edicts contradicting the Supreme
Soviet’s acts.

The pretext used was a clause in the Law on the
Supreme Soviet, whereby the quorum for opening
a session was specified as two-thirds of the elected
deputies. Since over the past five years the number of
parliamentarians has been gradually diminishing (some
moved to the executive branch, others left the country),
this resulted in a reduction in the Supreme Soviet's
quorum. The claimants, however, insisted that the figure
should remain unchanged; to their mind, the reference
point should be the number of deputies elected in 1990.

Meanwhile, there is no force in the republic that might
be able to counteract the outrages committed by the ex-
ecutive branch. On 11 October, for the first time, the
country’s only independent daily newspaper, NAROD-
NAYA VOLYA, did not appear. Tlie newspaper dared
to criticize the president. As a result, the state-owned
printing house where it was printed was instructed, with-
Out previous notice, to cancel its contract with the edi-
torial board. According to some information, the same
lot awaits other publications soon, too.

FBIS-SOV-95-199
16 October 1995

The presidential entourage could possibly regard the
Constitutional Court's ruling as a serious affront. This,
however, will hardly be able to alter the political balance
in the republic. Indeed, the story of Mr. Lukashenka’s
edict that was annulled by the Constitutional Court
ended with Premier Mikhail Chyhir’s statement that he
would be guided by the edict rather than by the court's
ruling — for reasons of “economic expediency.”

Snegur, Kohl Sign Agreement To Promote Relations

AU1410183895 Chisinau INFOTAG in English
1930 GMT 12 Oct 95

(FBIS Transcribed Text] Chisinau, October 12 (INFO-
TAG) — Moldovan President Mircea Snegur and Ger-
man Federal Chancellor Helmut Kohl signed the Dec-
laration on the Relationship Principles between the two
nations in Bonn last Wednesday [11 October].

According to the presidential press service, both coun-
tries have undertook to develop bilateral relations on the
basis of principles of international law, recognition of
equality of rights, sovereignty, territorial integrity and
inviolability of boundaries, human rights, including na-
tional minorities’ rights.

Answering the chancellor's question about the per-
spectives of Russian troops withdrawal from the
Moldovan territory, Mircea Snegur has pointed out
that the Moldovan administration has been finding
understanding with the Russian president and prime
minister. However, there are conservative forces in
the State Duma which back the Tiraspol authorities in
their attempts to establish an independent state in the
Moldova's eastern districts.

Currently, a new Moldovan-Russian summit is being
prepared. It will be dedicated to discussing the differ-
ences pertaining to the Dniester Region. Snegur ex-
pressed satisfaction that the Russian Federation had
ceased rendering assistance to the Tiraspol leaders and
builds relationship with Moldovan in a centralized way,
through legitimate power bodies in Chisinau.

Chancellor Kohl has assured his Moidovan counterpart
that the republic can count on Germany's support in
international organizations.

Besides the basic declaration, the sides signed agree-
ments on cultural cooperation, on taking care over the
German soldiers’ graves on the Moldovan territory, on
international automotive transportation of goods and

passengers.

WESTERN REGION 79

At his meeting with the German minister of foreign
affairs Klaus Kinkel, Snegur informed him on the
priorities in the Moldovan foreign policy. The Moldovan
president expressed readiness to back Germany in its
becoming a member of the UN Security Council, and
in making German one of the working languages in
the Council of Europe. In Kinkel’s words, the Dniester
settlement is important not only for Moldova but for
the entire Europe, and Germany is ready to render
every assistance to Chisinau in resolving the problem,
particularly in the OSCE framework. He said, Germany
is ready to stand up for giving a developing nation status
to Moldova, which will allow Chisinau to obtain credits
from developed industrial countries.

On Thursday, President Snegur met with leaders of
the Bundestag, Federal Ministry of Finance, German
Confederation of Chambers for Trade and Industry, and
the Dresdner Bank. Mircea Snegur answered journalists’
questions at a press conference in Bonn.

menting state support for newspapers, magazines, and
book-printing, the Ministry of Press and Mass Informa-
tion has been abolished By a presidential edict, and the
National Agency for Press and Mass Information of the
Republic of Kazakhstan has been formed.

By an edict of the president of the Republic of Kaza-
khstan, Altynbek Sarsenbayevich Sarsenbayev, who was
hitherto minister of press and mass information, has
been appointed chairman of the National Agency for
Press and Mass Information of the republic.

In order to deepen democratic processes and the pro-
vision of objective information to the citizens of the
r olic about the political, economic, social, and cul-
tural life of the country, to grant citizens their consti-
tutional rights to freedom of speech, and to implement
public control over the state mass media, an Observa-
tion Council on the State Mass Media has been set up
under the president.

Akayev Receives Deputy Prime Minister

LD1310195095 Moscow INTERFAX in English
1741 GMT 13 Oct 95

{From the "Presidential Bulletin" feature: compiled by
Nikolay Zherebtsov, Andrey Petrovskiy, and edited by
Viadimir Shishlin)

{FBIS Transcribed Text] President Askar Akayev re-
ceived from his Russian counterpart Boris Yeltsin on
Thursday a message with proposals to expand and per-
fect common border guarding cooperation.

Reporting this to journalists in Bishkek on Friday,
Akayev said that bilateral contacts in the field can
become “a model for CIS as a whole.” The president
felt it necessary to recall that after CIS formation
Kyrgyzstan was the sole former Soviet republic to keep
Russian border troops on its soil.

is report may contain copyrighted material. Copying and dissemination
1s prohibited without permission of the copyright owners.

82 CENTRAL ASIA

IF Note: At a news conference in Bishkek on Wednes-
day, Akayev actually supported Russia’s strategic
course toward closer integration within CIS and ap-
proved the decree issued by Yeltsin on September
14, noting that CIS should have common defense and
commen borders. At that news conference, Akayev
voiced his country’s firm support for a customs alliance
within CIS.

When Akayev and Russia’s Deputy Prime Minister
Aleksey Bolshakov met in Bishkek on Friday, they
discussed urgent bilateral ties issues and prospects of
economic cooperation between their countries.

Bolshakov is in Kyrgyzstan on a working visit at the
head of a Russian government delegation.

On the same day, the sides signed a package of
documents on bilateral ties, including an agreement on
the basic principles of setting up financial and industrial
groups, on cooperation in the field of industrial property
protection, and on the use of Kyrgyzstan’s proper
for settling accounts with Russia. They also signed a
protocol on the formation of a customs union and a set
of agreements on maintaining the Russian servicemen,
serving in Kyrgyzstan.

Bolshakov believes the signed documents create a
legal basis for still closer cooperation between the two
countries, above all economically. Bolshakov noted that
the entire work of the sides in the field aims to draft a
customs union agreement and put it into effect. Once
such a document is adopted, “all far-fetched problems,
above all the custom barriers, will be eliminated to pave
the way for free commoaities exchange."

Bolshakov regards as immensely important for the two
countries an agreement to start joint production at
enterprises of the former military industrial complex.
He also reported that from now on part of Kyrgyz debts
to Russia would be offset by shares of local industries,
involving Russian investment. According to Bolshakov,
there is a plan to draw up by the end of this year a
list of enterprises capable of manufacturing competitive
products, enjoying demand on the world market. That
would be “for the mutual benefit of both sides."

As part of its visit to Kyrgyzstan, the Russian delegation
is to make a trip to that republic’s Issyk-Kul region,
where it will visit a former munitions plant for the
production of high precision torpedoes.

Tajikistan
CIS Peacekeeping Commander Defends
Deployment of Brigade

LD1310195595 Moscow INTERFAX in English
1741 GMT 13 Oct 95

[From the "Presidential Bulletin" feature: compiled by
Nikolay Zherebtsov and Andrey Petrovskiy, edited by
Viadimir Shishlin)

{FBIS Transcribed Text] The commander of a collective
Commonwealth of Independent States peacekeeping
force in Tajikistan denied on Friday that deploying
a Tajik army brigade in the Vakhsh Valley and the
foothills of the Pamirs recently ordered by President
Emomali Rakhmanov was a breach of a provisional

CENTRAL ASIA 83

cease-fire signed between the Tajik government and
opposition in Tehran.

IF Note: The leader of the opposition Islamic Renais-
sance Movement of Tajikistan, Said Abdullo Nuri, sent
a letter to Rakhmanov on October 11, accusing him of
blocking talks between the government and opposition
and described the transfer of the First Special-Purpose
Brigade from Kurgan-Tyube into the Vakhsh Valley and
the Pamir foothills as an "inappropriate and hasty" act
which violated the Tehran accord.

The peacekeepers’ commander, Colonel General
Valentin Bobryshev, told a news conference in
Dushanbe that he saw the redeployment as no more
than "a commensurate response by the Tajik leadership
to what is being undertaken by the opposition.”

He conceded, however, that moving the brigade had
been “undesirable” and made the peacekeepers’ tasks
"more difficult" in spite of the fact that "relevant
statements” had been made before the redeployment was
effected.

While he expressed "concern" over the postponement
of the next round of talks between government and op-
position, which was due to start on September 18, Bo-
bryshev argued that the main reason for the delay were
“unacceptable” statements the opposition made during
the last, fourth round, statements which "effectively dis-
own everything the government of Tajikistan has done
over the postwar years.”

The general expressed the conviction, however, that the
parties would resume their dialogue. The peacekeeping
force is ready to assist the negotiation process "as far
as its powers allow."

Further on Bobryshev News Conference

LD1310164395 Moscow INTERFAX in English
1323 GMT 13 Oct 95

{#BIS Transcribed Text} DUSHANBE, Oct 13 (Inter-
fax) — Russia alone has honored its commitments to
the collective peacekeeping force in Tajikistan, Colonel
General Valentin Bobryshev, the force commander, said
at a news conference in Dushanbe on the second an-
niversary of the force’s formation.

According to Bobryshev, Uzbekistan and Kyrgyzstan,
other parties to the treaty on the force, sent in a battalion
and a company, respectively, instead of a regiment each.
He went on to say that Kazakhstan is represented by
only two officers in the force headquarters who attend to
the Kyrgyz battalion subordinated to the Russian border
guards in Tajikistan.

is report may contain copyrighted material. Copying and dissemination

&4 CENTRAL ASIA

Bobryshev said that in the two years of the force’s
operation 75 peacekeeping soldiers have been killed.

He said that the situation on the Tajik-Afghan border
has significantly deteriorated. Bobryshev added that a
band of about 250 fighters has moved inside Tajikistan
from the Vanch district to the central part of the plateau
area.

Bobryshev did not rule out the possibility that his units
could be involved in military operations in that area
or on the border with Afghanistan if hostilities break
out there. He said that this will require the political
will of the heads of the states whose units make up the
collective peacekeeping force.

Seminar on Preservation of Aral Sea Continues

LD1410110295 Moscow ITAR-TASS World Service
in Russian 0902 GMT 14 Oct 95

[By ITAR-TASS correspondent Vilor Niyazmatov}

{[FBIS Translated Text] Tashkent, 14 Oct — An inter-
national seminar “Restoration of the Environment”, held
under OSCE auspices, completed its work in Tashkent
today. It was here where the seminar started its work
last Tuesday and later the seminar continued its work
in the town of Urgench in northern Uzbekistan. Those
present at the seminar worked out guidelines, which, in
their opinion, will help ta.draw the world community's
attention to the ecological crisis in the Aral Sea area,
which poses a serious danger not only to the inhabi-
tants of the Central Asian states but also to European
and Asian countries.

Wilhelm Hoynck, Secretary-General of the OSCE, who
attended the meeting, pointed out that we have to use
additional arguments in favour of the support which is
above all urgently needed for the Central Asian states.

Scientists and experts pointed out with alarm in their
speeches that a process of a sharp reduction in the size
of ice fields is taking place at present. This can seriously
influence the water resources of the Central Asian region
and will aggravate the ecological crisis in the Aral Sea
basin.

FBIS-SOV-95-199

“The forum was held in Central Asia," Archbishop

CAUCASUS 87

Figures Given on Russian Assumption of Debt

NC1510133295 Yerevan NOYAN TAPAN in English
1638 GMT 13 Oct 95

(FBIS Transcribed Text] On October 11, National As-
sembly of Armenia ratified the Moscow agreement on
external debts and assets of the former USSR signed be-
tween Armenia and Russian Federation on September 3,
1993. Under this agreement Russia assumes responsibil-
ity to pay off Armenia's share in USSR’s external debt.
As of December 1, 1991 Armenia's share in former
USSR’s external debt was equivalent to 590 min USD.
According to data provided by Paris Official Creditors
Club, process of paying off the debt and interests should
have started in 1992 in accordance with the following
time-table (in millions of dollars):

Years Sum to be paid off Interests to be paid off
1992 145.3 39.3%
1993 122.1 40.5%
1994 147.1 39.4%
1995 135.3 29.1%

According to Armenian Finance Ministry, Armenia like
other CIS countries is incapable of paying off these
sums because of their great size. Russian Federation
agreed to pay off these sums demanding in exchange
that CIS countries refuse from assets of the former
USSR abroad. Since there is certain difficulties in re-
turning these assets, all republics of the former Soviet
Union decided to give them up and concluded agree-
ments with Russia, according to “zero variant”.

If Armenia had refused from this agreement, in late 1995
it would have had to pay off 850 min USD. Payment of
interests and this debt would have constituted over 200
min USD per year.

Armenian Finance Ministry emphasized that Armenia's
current external debt does not exceed even 1/3 of the
above-mentioned sum.

President Promises Cease-Fire Observance in

LD1210213795 Moscow INTERFAX in English

[From the “Diplomatic Panorama” feature}

{[FBIS Transcribed Text] BAKU, Oct 12 (Interfax) —
President Geydar Aliyev of Azerbaijan has confirmed
his country’s determination to observe ceasefire “up
to the complete settlement of the Karabakh conflict.”
He made this statement meeting in Baku the State

Secretary for Matters of the Swiss Confederation Jakob
Kellenberg.

Switzerland will take over chairmanship from Hungary
in 1996 of the OSCE, which is mediating settlement of
the Karabakh conflict.

FBIS-SOV-95-199
16 October 1995

The Azeri president said that Baku hopes for “achieving
concrete results” at the next round of the Armenian-
Azeri talks which is being held at the Aland Islands
(Finland) under the auspices of the OSCE Minsk Group
on Nagorno Karabakh.

“We hope that the Minsk Group will manage to achieve
progress in the settlement process before the end of the
year,” said Aliyev.

For his part, Kellenberg said that Switzerland, recogniz-
ing the territorial integrity of Azerbaijan, believes that
the Azeri lands occupied by Armenians must be liber-
ated.

IMF Pian for Economy Criticized as ‘Scam’

MM1610094895 Moscow ROSSIYSKAYA GAZETA
in Russian 14 Oct 95 p 14

[Article by A. Vladikavkazskiy: “What It Would Mean
If Sellers Outnumbered Buyers. Economic Crisis in
Azerbaijan and How It Was Provoked by IMF Block-
headedness"]

(FBIS Translated Text] Baku — The model of economic
renewal in Azerbaijan proposed by the IMF has been
dubbed the “scam of the century” by independent
economists. It is by no means a question of a turn
of phrase: the specialists are working on the basis
of realities — production has virtually ground to a
halt in the country, with the possible exception of
the still-functioning oil fields and a small number
of plants and factories, and the population has been
impoverished. And this is largely the consequence of the
Capriciousness of this not very compliant international
organization.

The IMF program is simple but tough — spend what
you earn and use your loans sensibly to modernize
industry and the agrarian sector. By following the IMF's
unquestioned demands, the government has reduced
bank lending to a minimum and has not increased
the minimum wage for 1! months. A kind of vicious
circle has formed — because of the lack of lending to
develop production, enterprises are standing idle and
are not paying taxes to the treasury, which has left
the government unable to pay pensions (its total debt
is up to 70 billion manats) or salaries to workers at
budget-sector organizations. In order to stop inflation
running riot the dollar exchange rate has been artificially
maintained — by selling petrodollars in the market.
Bonuses and interim loans intended for other purposes
have been used.

In order to explain the unenviable situation in which
the country finds itself we need only recognize that it
is hard to revive the economic administrative system

is report may contain copyrighted maternal. Copying and dissemination
is prohibited without permission of the copyright owners.

FBIS-SOV-95-199
16 October 1995

that collapsed under the People’s Front regime. Yes,
this is indeed so. President Geydar Aliyev has recently
reiterated that the Nagorno-Karabakh war and the more
than 1 million refugees on the republic’s territory are
having a negative impact on the economic situation.
Naturally, the disruption to production ties with the CIS
States, the restrictions on North Caucasus rail traffic as
a result of the events in Chechnya, the repeated coup
attempts, and so forth are also having an effect.

You would have thought that such a combination of
circumstances was just a matter of time — everything
takes time, as they say. Total loans in 1995, including
IMF credits, come to $400 million. That is not counting
the bonus allocated by the International Oil Consortium.
This contribution came to $200 million. Ministry of
Foreign Economic Relations experts claim that every
dollar invested in production today will be returned five-
fold in the next three- five years taking account of the
high quality of output that should find outlets on the
world market. Petrodollars will be a special item of
future revenue. Oil exports from the Chirag-1 oil field
will be producing sizable profits early next year.

The prodlem is whether the country’s population is
prepared to starve and go poor until such time as
the economy picks up — which will take at least a
whole decade. The resources of patience in the social
mentality are limited and, by all accounts, are long since
exhausted. According to state statistics, the consumer
basket currently costs 248,865 manats. The government
is planning to increase the minimum wage by 15 percent
(or 6,300 manats, $1.20- 1.40) as of 1 October. Even
then the minimum wage will not exceed 3 percent of
the overall costs of the consumer basket.

In order to assess the situation it is easier to work things
out in rubles — the Azerbaijani manat has long been
comparable to the Russian currency and has approx-
imately the same dollar exchange rate. But prices in
the Azerbaijani market exceed Moscow prices, which
means that it is beyond nature for the republic's author-
ities to expect the population to live on 6,300 rubles.
That level of benefit will be paid to nonworking refugees
and displaced persons. The average wage varies between
15,000 and 60,000 manats. This imbalance arose not of
its own accord but as a result of a definite metamorpho-
sis — when the national currency was introduced the
manat exchange rate against the Russian ruble was set
at 1-10. This allowed the authors of monetary reform
to exploit the bungling of the People’s Front leaders
and reduce tenfold the value of state property, housing,
and other assets that the people had built up over many
decades. And it allowed them to sell off the people's
assets for dollars — to their own advantage, of course
— and leave everyone else holding the baby. The fact

CAUCASUS 89

is that the governments that have followed the People’s
Front regime have begun to equalize prices, using the
dollar as an equivalent. They have equalized everything
apart from wages, which are still at the "one-tenth" level.
Unfortunately, the crushing edict on the correlation be-
tween the manat and the Russian ruble that was signed
three years ago by former President Abulfaz Elchibey
has not been repealed.

This is what it is like to live guided by the directions of
the IMF, which refuses to “authorize” a sharp increase
in the minimum wage because it has not been earned.
But how can it be earned when enterprises eking out
a miserable existence are standing idle? The ordinary
people are taking no account of this, if statistics are
to be believed — the total number of small traders
has exceeded the total number of buyers over the past
year. IMF specialists, admitting that this phenomenon
is rarely encountered, put it down to the "specifics of
national economic relations.” Boxes of prime necessities
are brought out in front of buildings, in public places,
or in subway underpasses to be sold. And the money
hardly ever finds its way into a bank — only 16 out of
every 100 manats do. To those 16 manats are added 84
manats — after the latest sale in the market of rapidly
disappearing bonus petrodollars....

Citibank To Fitiance Oil Field Development

MM1310130495 Moscow IZVESTIYA (Financial
Section) in Russian 13 Oct 95 pl

[AGENTSTVO NEFTYANOY INFORMATSII report:
“Azerbaijan To Continue Developing Inaccessidle Oil
Fields With American Money"]}

[FBIS Translated Text] America’s Citibank intends to
continue financing two projects to develop inaccessible
deposits in oil fields in Azerbaijan with an estimated
total value of $300 million. The contract, funded by
an American bank, was concluded on the basis of
production sharing and includes prospecting work in the
western part of the Apsheron Peninsula, as well as the
development of known deposits in the northern wing of
the Karadagskaya [name as transliterated] structure and
in the Kergez [name as transliterated] Field. Planned
total investment under both sections of the contract
is around $300 million over the course of 25 years.
Estimated oil reserves in known areas are put at around
42 million tonnes, and at 21 million tonnes in the
developed sector.

Legal Proceedings Continue Against Journalists
LD1310205795 Moscow INTERFAX in English
1927 GMT 13 Oct 95

[FBIS Transcribed Text] BAKU, Oct 13 (Inter“ax) —
Legal proceedings concerning four journalists continued
in the Baku Municipal Court today. The journalists
are charged with publishing in the Chemshe (Reed)
self-published satirical newspaper articles offending the
honor and dignity of the Azerbaijani president.

The prosecutor demanded tonight that journalist Ayaz
Akhmedov and journalist Yadigara Mamedli be sen-
tenced to five and three years of imprisonment respec-
tively. A three-year sentence was proposed for journal-
ists Asker Akhmed and Malik Bairamov.

The journalists maintain their innocence saying that the
legal proceedings are of political nature.

An Interfax correspondent reports that the accused
remained handcuffed during the legal proceedings,
chained to each other.

No video taping or photographs were allowed during
the legal proceedings.

"The authorities are trying to intimidate the free media
by these proceedings and fan the atmosphere of depres-
sion on the eve of parliamentary elections," said Chair-
man of the Independent Democratic Party of Azerbai-
jan, Chairman of the Human Rights Committee Leila
Yunusova.

The Azerbaijani Musavat party’s press center said the
Reporters Without Borders international human rights
organization for journalists had sent on October 10
an appeal to Azerbaijani President Heydar Aliyev.
Reporters Without Borders said they were seriously
concerned about the legal proceedings against the four
journalists. "No accusation of offending the honor and
dignity of the president can exist in democratic countries
which defend freedom of the media," Reporters Without
Borders said.

Georgia
Former Security Chief Blames Russia for Terrorist
Acts

LD1310163795 Tbilisi IPRINDA in Russian
1230 GMT 13 Oct 95

{FBIS Translated Text] Tbilisi, 13 Oct — Irakli Bati-
ashvili, the former chief of the Information and Intelli-
gence Service of Georgia, held a news conference today
devoted to the terrorist acts that were recently carried
out in the republic.

Batiashvili said that these terrorist acts were not ac-
cidental at all. "Caucasus has turned into an arena of

FBIS-SOV-95-199
16 October 1995

confrontation between big powers and the events that
took place have to be viewed from this angle."

A great number of these terrorist acts have been
commissioned by the Russian Security Service, as
Russia can feel the growing interest of Turkey, the
United States, and other states in Georgia.

The terrorist act, which was carried out on 29 August,
ahead of a visit to Georgia by Turkish Prime Minister
Tansu Ciller, was meant as a warning to Turkey; and the
killing of the U.S. embassy employee Fred Woodruff,
which preceded a visit to Georgia by CIA Chairman
Woolsey, was targeted as a warning to the United States.
As for the terrorist act of 29 August, it was a result
of a subversive act by the General Staff of the main
intelligence service of Russia.

According to Batiashvili, the Georgian leadership is well
aware of all this but they remain silent. As for Igor
Giorgadze, the Russian side will never extradite him to
Georgian authorities.

Tbilisi Negotiates Treaty on Cooperation With EU
LD1310184595 Moscow INTERFAX in English
1602 GMT 13 Oct 95

[From the "Diplomatic Panorama” feature]

{FBIS Transcribed Text] TBILISI, Oct 13 (Interfax)
— The relations between the E.U. and Georgia are
acquiring a qualitatively new nature, Georgia’s First
Deputy Foreign Minister Mikhail Ukleba told journalists
upon his return to Tbilisi Thursday. He headed the
Georgian delegation to the first round of talks on a large-
scale bilateral treaty on friendship and cooperation in
Brussels on October 9-10.

Ukleba said the E.U. had regarded Georgia as an area to
deliver humanitarian aid to until recently. Now the E.U.
assistance program aims to promote economic reforms
in the republic.

According to Ukleba, the treaty will constitute "the po-
litical and economic backbone” of E.U-Georgian rela-
tions. The documents provides for cooperation in in-
vestments, industry, agriculture, transport, energy pro-
duction, telecommunications, finances and other areas.

Ukleba said the treaty also contained some provisions
on granting the republic the most-favored-nation status
on Georgia’s initiative.

Ukleba said the next round of talks will be held this
November or December to sign the treaty.

Abkhazia Asks UN To Help Extradite Murder
Suspects

LD1410153095 Sukhumi Republic of Abkhazia Radio
in Russian 0510 GMT 14 Oct 95

(FBIS Translated Excerpt] Mr. Liviu Bota, deputy to
the UN secretary general’s special envoy to Georgia,

CAUCASUS 91

continues his visit to Abkhazia. On 12 October he was
received at the Prosecutor’s Office of the Republic of
Abkhazia.

The Abkhaz Prosecutor’s Office asked Mr. Liviu Bota
to mediate in the Abkhaz side’s attempt to extradite
the notorious Tsveyba brothers, who were involved in
the [September 1995] assassination of Deputy Prime
Minister Yuriy Voronov.

We are sure that they were involved in the assassi-
nation and we have specific evidence supporting this,
Prosecutor-General Anri Jergenia said. In Anri Jerge-
nia’s opinion, the Georgian side, which has more than
once in the past refused or ignored similar demands
by Abkhazia, should observe internationally recognized
norms and extradite the Tsveyba brothers.

Mr. Bota expressed readiness to help Abkhazia in this
question. [passage omitted]

The prosecutor general of Abkhazia presented Mr. Liviu
Bota with relevant documents and asked him to hand
them over to the Georgian authorities.

Polls Indicate Shevardnadze Supported by 55
Percent

LD1310191395 Tbilisi CONTACT INFORMATION
AGENCY in Russian 1700 GMT 13 Oct 95

{FBIS Translated Text] Tbilisi — According to the
Statistical data obtained as a result of polls, which are
carried out regularly by the mass media, one can draw
the conclusion that nearly 55 percent of population
supports the candidacy of the Georgian head of state
(Eduard Shevardnadze] for the post of president of the
country.

His strongest contender Jumber Patiashvili has the
support of only 17 percent, and no more than 3 to
4 percent of the population supports the other four
candidates for president.

As for the parliamentary elections, the Georgian
National-Democratic party comes first and is followed
by the Union of Citizens of Georgia.
